Source
Neutral status. The abc conjecture is not proved here. The project neither assumes that inter-universal Teichmüller theory is correct nor that it is wrong; a countermodel to a simplified abstraction is not a counterexample to the papers.

Statements

The statements of IUT I to IV: 369 statements in the mechanical index (numbered and lettered), 17 curated unnumbered items of a section, and 88 curated parts and proof steps. Each row is a statement of the mechanical index or a curated unnumbered item of a section; its curated parts, proof steps and fragments are listed in its record.

blocked by missing justification (BLOCKED_BY_MISSING_JUSTIFICATION): the text gives an assertion where a formalization needs a derivation; this is not a claim that the result is false.

The table is data/statements.json, the mechanical index merged with the curated records of data/curated/. Titles come from the mechanical extraction: subscripts and superscripts are often lost, and a few titles keep traces of symbols. Statement status not extracted means that the statement is not yet recorded to the standard of an extracted statement (content, hypotheses and conclusion from pages read); the record may already hold partial content. A Lean declaration is shown for a record only when the record names one; a fragment checks one step, and its parent is not counted as formalized through it.

IUT1.ThmATheorem AF± l -/Fl -Symmetries, Θ-Links, and Frobenius-/Étale-Pictures Associated to Θ±ell NF-Hodge Theaters
Record

Construction of Θ^{±ell}NF-Hodge theaters from initial Θ-data, with an F_l^{⋊±}-symmetry on the additive side and an F_l^*-symmetry on the multiplicative side; the Θ-link from the Θ-pilot data of one theater to the q-pilot data of another, which induces a full poly-isomorphism of the unit portions; Frobenius-pictures and étale-pictures.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • A fixed collection of initial Θ-data (Definition 3.1).

Conclusion.

Summary statement of Corollaries 3.7 to 3.9, 4.12, 6.10, 6.12 and Definition 6.13 in introductory form.

Obligations.

  • Every term needs a definition: Frobenioids, D-prime-strips, Hodge theaters, poly-isomorphisms.

Ambiguities.

  • Introductory summary; the precise content is in the corollaries of §§3 to 6.

IUT1.ThmBTheorem BProfinite Conjugates of Tempered Decomposition and Inertia Groups
Record

Let k be a mixed-characteristic nonarchimedean local field and X a hyperbolic curve over k, with tempered fundamental group Π^tp_X and étale fundamental group Π̂_X, so that Π^tp_X ⊆ Π̂_X and Π̂_X is the profinite completion of Π^tp_X. (1) A decomposition group in Π̂_X of a closed point or cusp of X (respectively an inertia group in Π̂_X of a cusp) is contained in Π^tp_X if and only if it is a decomposition group in Π^tp_X of a closed point or cusp (respectively an inertia group in Π^tp_X of a cusp). (2) A Π̂_X-conjugate of Π^tp_X contains a decomposition group in Π^tp_X of a closed point or cusp (respectively an inertia group in Π^tp_X of a cusp) if and only if it is equal to Π^tp_X.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • k a mixed-characteristic nonarchimedean local field (MLF)
  • X a hyperbolic curve over k
  • Π^tp_X the tempered fundamental group and Π̂_X the étale fundamental group of X, relative to suitable basepoints, with the natural inclusion Π^tp_X ⊆ Π̂_X

Conclusion.

(1) For every decomposition group D ⊆ Π̂_X of a closed point or cusp (respectively inertia group I ⊆ Π̂_X of a cusp): D ⊆ Π^tp_X if and only if D is a decomposition group in Π^tp_X of a closed point or cusp (respectively I ⊆ Π^tp_X if and only if I is an inertia group in Π^tp_X of a cusp). (2) For every γ ∈ Π̂_X: γ·Π^tp_X·γ^{-1} contains some decomposition group in Π^tp_X of a closed point or cusp (respectively some inertia group in Π^tp_X of a cusp) if and only if γ·Π^tp_X·γ^{-1} = Π^tp_X.

Obligations.

  • Tempered fundamental groups are not in Mathlib.

Ambiguities.

  • Page 16 says Theorem B is essentially Corollary 2.5 (with Remark 2.5.2), and that the stable reduction hypothesis of Corollary 2.5 is unnecessary by Proposition 2.4 (iii). This is a relation between the two statements, not part of Theorem B; Theorem B itself has no stable reduction hypothesis.

IUT1.Def1_1Definition 1.1
Record

Notation: Xu, Cu denote X, C with an underline in the paper; X→, C→ the arrow-decorated curves. Setting of page 37: l ≥ 5 an integer prime to 6; X a hyperbolic curve of type (1,1) over a field k of characteristic zero; Cu a hyperbolic orbicurve of type (1, l-tors)± over k whose k-core C also forms a k-core of X; Xu = Cu ×_C X, of type (1, l-tors); assumption (∗): G_k acts trivially on Δ_{Xu}^ab ⊗ Z/lZ. Pages 37 and 38 choose a nonzero cusp ε of Cu, use the decomposition group of the cusp 2ε to get a section σ : G_k → J_{Cu}, and obtain finite étale cyclic coverings X→ → Xu and C→ → Cu through cartesian diagrams, with Gal(C→/Cu) ≅ Z/lZ. Definition 1.1: a hyperbolic orbicurve over k isomorphic to the X→ (respectively C→) so constructed, for some choice of l and ε, is of type (1, l-tors→) (respectively (1, l-tors→)±).

Explicit hypotheses.

  • k a field of characteristic zero
  • l ≥ 5 an integer prime to 6 (page 37; l is not required to be prime here)
  • X a hyperbolic curve of type (1,1) over k; Cu a hyperbolic orbicurve of type (1, l-tors)± over k ([EtTh] Definition 2.1) whose k-core C is also a k-core of X; Xu = Cu ×_C X
  • assumption (∗): the natural action of G_k on Δ_{Xu}^ab ⊗ (Z/lZ) is trivial
  • ε a nonzero cusp of Cu

Conclusion.

Definition (no proof obligation): it names the types (1, l-tors→) and (1, l-tors→)± of the orbicurves X→ and C→ constructed on pages 37 and 38.

Obligations.

  • Orbicurves (stacks) are not in Mathlib.

Ambiguities.

  • The arrow decoration and the underlines were read from the rendered pages 37 to 39; Remark 1.1.1 (page 39) says the arrow denotes the archimedean ordered labels 1, 2, ... determined by the choice of ε.

IUT1.Cor1_2Corollary 1.2Characteristic Nature of Coverings
Record

Notation as in IUT1.Def1_1 (Xu, Cu underlined; X→, C→ arrow-decorated). Suppose k is an NF or an MLF. There is a functorial group-theoretic algorithm that reconstructs Π_{Xu}, Π_{C→}, Π_{Cu} (respectively Π_{Cu}) together with the conjugacy classes of the decomposition groups determined by the sets of cusps {ε′, ε″}; {ε} (respectively {ε}) from Π_{X→} (respectively Π_{C→}); functoriality is with respect to isomorphisms of topological groups, and the groups are reconstructed as subgroups of Aut(Π_{X→}) (respectively Aut(Π_{C→})).

Explicit hypotheses.

  • k an NF or an MLF
  • the setting of Section 1, pages 37 and 38

Conclusion.

Existence of a functorial group-theoretic algorithm reconstructing Π_{Xu}, Π_{C→}, Π_{Cu} (respectively Π_{Cu}) and the stated conjugacy classes of decomposition groups of cusps from Π_{X→} (respectively Π_{C→}), as subgroups of Aut(Π_{X→}) (respectively Aut(Π_{C→})).

Obligations.

  • Étale fundamental groups of orbicurves; notion of group-theoretic algorithm.

Ambiguities.

  • "Algorithm" is informal; no formal model of computation is specified.

IUT1.Prop2_1Proposition 2.1Profinite Conjugates of Nontrivial Compact Subgroups
Record

In the notation of the preceding discussion, let Λ ⊆ Π^tp_G be a nontrivial compact subgroup and γ ∈ Π̂_G with γ·Λ·γ^{-1} ⊆ Π^tp_G (equivalently Λ ⊆ γ^{-1}·Π^tp_G·γ). Then γ ∈ Π^tp_G.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • the notation of the discussion preceding Proposition 2.1 (a semi-graph of anabelioids G with tempered group Π^tp_G ⊆ Π̂_G)
  • Λ ⊆ Π^tp_G a nontrivial compact subgroup
  • γ ∈ Π̂_G with γ·Λ·γ^{-1} ⊆ Π^tp_G

Conclusion.

γ ∈ Π^tp_G.

Obligations.

  • Semi-graphs of anabelioids and tempered groups are not in Mathlib.

Ambiguities.

  • Read at the level of statements only; proofs not checked.

IUT1.Prop2_2Proposition 2.2Commensurators of Decomposition Subgroups Associated to Sub-semi-graphs
Record

In the notation of the preceding discussion, Π̂_H (respectively Π^tp_H), the decomposition subgroup associated to a sub-semi-graph H, is commensurably terminal in Π̂_G (respectively in Π̂_G, hence also in Π^tp_G). In particular Π^tp_G is commensurably terminal in Π̂_G.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • the notation of the discussion preceding Proposition 2.1: a semi-graph of anabelioids G, a sub-semi-graph H, decomposition subgroups Π^tp_H ⊆ Π^tp_G and Π̂_H ⊆ Π̂_G

Conclusion.

Π̂_H is commensurably terminal in Π̂_G; Π^tp_H is commensurably terminal in Π̂_G and in Π^tp_G; Π^tp_G is commensurably terminal in Π̂_G.

Obligations.

  • Semi-graphs of anabelioids and tempered groups are not in Mathlib.

Ambiguities.

  • Read at the level of statements only; proofs not checked.

IUT1.Cor2_5Corollary 2.5Profinite Conjugates of Tempered Decomposition and Inertia Groups
Record

In the notation of the preceding discussion (Proposition 2.4), with Σ̂ = Primes: every decomposition group in Π̂_X of a closed point or cusp (respectively inertia group in Π̂_X of a cusp) is contained in Π^tp_X if and only if it is a decomposition group (respectively inertia group) in Π^tp_X of a closed point or cusp (respectively cusp); and a Π̂_X-conjugate of Π^tp_X contains a decomposition group (respectively inertia group) in Π^tp_X of a closed point or cusp (respectively cusp) if and only if it equals Π^tp_X.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • the notation of the discussion preceding Proposition 2.4 (pages 49 and 50, not rendered; it includes a stable model, cf. page 16)
  • Σ̂ = Primes

Conclusion.

The two equivalences above, the second with an existential quantifier over decomposition (inertia) groups contained in the conjugate.

Obligations.

  • Semi-graphs of anabelioids and tempered groups are not in Mathlib.

Ambiguities.

  • The setting of Proposition 2.4 (pages 49 and 50) was read from the text layer only; page 16 says the stable reduction assumption of this corollary is unnecessary.

IUT1.Thm2_6Theorem 2.6Profinite Conjugates of Discrete Subgroups
Record

Let F be a group containing a finite index subgroup G that is a free discrete group of finite rank or the fundamental group of a compact orientable topological surface of genus ≥ 2, and H ⊆ F an infinite subgroup. Regard H, G ⊆ F ⊆ F̂ (F is residually finite), F̂ the profinite completion. Let γ ∈ F̂ with γ·H·γ^{-1} ⊆ F. Write H_G = H ∩ G. Then γ ∈ F·N_F̂(H_G), that is, γ·H_G·γ^{-1} = δ·H_G·δ^{-1} for some δ ∈ F. If moreover H_G is nonabelian, then γ ∈ F.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • G ⊆ F of finite index, G free of finite rank or orientable surface group of genus ≥ 2
  • H ⊆ F infinite
  • γ ∈ F̂ with γHγ^{-1} ⊆ F

Implicit hypotheses.

  • F residually finite, cited from [Config] Proposition 7.1 (ii).

Conclusion.

γ ∈ F·N_F̂(H_G), the normalizer in F̂ of H_G itself (not of its closure); equivalently γ·H_G·γ^{-1} = δ·H_G·δ^{-1} for some δ ∈ F. If H_G is nonabelian, γ ∈ F.

Obligations.

  • Statable in Lean: Mathlib has free groups and profinite completion of groups in some form; surface groups would need a definition. Not attempted.

Ambiguities.

  • Page 56 (rendered) shows N_F̂(H_G) with H_G undecorated; the closure Ĥ_G appears only in the proof on page 57, in N_Ĝ(H_G) = Ĥ_G.

IUT1.Lem2_7Lemma 2.7Well-known Properties of Free Groups and Orientable Surface Groups
Record

Let G be as in Theorem 2.6 (free of finite rank, or an orientable surface group of genus ≥ 2) and Ĝ its profinite completion. (i) Any subgroup of G generated by two elements is free. (ii) For x ∈ G, x ≠ 1, there is a finite index subgroup G_1 ⊆ G with x ∈ G_1 and x of nontrivial image in G_1^ab. (iii) For noncommuting x, y ∈ G there are a finite index subgroup G_1 ⊆ G and n ≥ 1 with x^n, y^n ∈ G_1 whose images in G_1^ab generate a free abelian subgroup of rank two. (iv) Every abelian subgroup of G is cyclic. (v) If T̂ ⊆ Ĝ is a closed subgroup and there is a continuous surjection Ĝ ↠ Ẑ inducing an isomorphism T̂ ≅ Ẑ, then T̂ is normally terminal in Ĝ. (vi) If G is nonabelian and N̂ = Ker(Ĝ ↠ Ĝ^ab), the centralizer Z_Ĝ(N̂) is trivial. (vii) In the notation of (vi), an automorphism of the profinite group Ĝ that preserves N̂ and restricts to the identity on N̂ is the identity.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • G a free discrete group of finite rank or the fundamental group of a compact orientable surface of genus ≥ 2 (as in Theorem 2.6)
  • Ĝ the profinite completion of G
  • (ii): x ∈ G, x ≠ 1
  • (iii): x, y ∈ G noncommuting
  • (v): T̂ ⊆ Ĝ closed, with a continuous surjection Ĝ ↠ Ẑ inducing T̂ ≅ Ẑ
  • (vi), (vii): G nonabelian; (vii): α an automorphism of Ĝ preserving N̂ with α|_N̂ = id

Conclusion.

(i) two-generated subgroups are free; (ii) separation of x ≠ 1 in the abelianization of a finite index subgroup; (iii) x^n, y^n span a rank two free abelian subgroup of G_1^ab; (iv) abelian subgroups are cyclic; (v) T̂ is normally terminal in Ĝ; (vi) Z_Ĝ(N̂) = {1}; (vii) α = id.

Obligations.

  • Several parts are classical facts whose Mathlib coverage was not checked; surface groups are not defined in Mathlib.

IUT1.Cor2_8Corollary 2.8Subgroups of Topological Fundamental Groups of Complex Hyperbolic Curves
Record

Let Z be a hyperbolic curve over ℂ, Π_Z its usual topological fundamental group, Π̂_Z the profinite completion, H ⊆ Π_Z an infinite subgroup (such as a cuspidal inertia group), and γ ∈ Π̂_Z with γ·H·γ^{-1} ⊆ Π_Z. Then γ ∈ Π_Z·N_{Π̂_Z}(H), i.e. γ·H·γ^{-1} = δ·H·δ^{-1} for some δ ∈ Π_Z. If moreover H is nonabelian, then γ ∈ Π_Z.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • Z a hyperbolic curve over ℂ
  • Π_Z the topological fundamental group of Z, Π̂_Z its profinite completion
  • H ⊆ Π_Z an infinite subgroup
  • γ ∈ Π̂_Z with γ·H·γ^{-1} ⊆ Π_Z

Conclusion.

γ ∈ Π_Z·N_{Π̂_Z}(H), equivalently γ·H·γ^{-1} = δ·H·δ^{-1} for some δ ∈ Π_Z; if H is nonabelian, γ ∈ Π_Z.

Obligations.

  • Topological fundamental groups of Riemann surfaces.

Ambiguities.

  • Remark 2.8.1 (page 59) says Corollary 2.8 is an immediate consequence of Theorem 2.6.

IUT1.Def3_1Definition 3.1
Record

Initial Θ-data: a tuple (F̄/F, X_F, l, C_K, V, V^bad_mod, ε) subject to conditions (a) to (f).

Conclusion.

Definition: no proof obligation.

Obligations.

  • Only clauses of (a), (b), (c) are stated in Lean, as fragments (see fragments).
  • Existence of initial Θ-data is asserted in IUT IV Corollary 2.2 (pages 41 and 42), not in IUT I.
  • No inhabitant of IUT.PaperI.PartialInitialThetaConditions is exhibited in Lean, so even the consistency of the formalized clauses is open. A witness needs a number field F containing √−1 and the full 6-torsion of E_F (hence, by the Weil pairing, a primitive cube root of unity, so F ⊇ Q(ζ_12); in particular F = Q(i) is impossible), semistable reduction of E_F at all finite places, a nonempty odd set S of places of k with multiplicative reduction above S, F/k Galois of degree prime to l, and a prime l ≥ 5 prime to the residue characteristics of S. See docs/INITIAL_THETA_DATA.md for a construction outline; no specific curve has been checked.

Ambiguities.

  • Remark 3.1.3 (page 65): the data is determined up to insignificant choices by (F̄/F, X_F, C_K, V, V^bad_mod); the choice of V is described as insignificant.

Pages read: 61 to 63 (text and rendered image)

      Pages read: 61 (text and rendered image)

  • IUT1.Def3_1.b (part or step) extracted not extracted
    Record

    X_F once-punctured elliptic curve over F with stable reduction at all nonarchimedean places; E_F its compactification; C_F the stack quotient by −1; F_mod the field of moduli of X_F; F_sol the maximal solvable extension of F_mod in F̄; V_mod = 𝕍(F_mod); V^bad_mod ⊆ V^non_mod a nonempty set of odd residue characteristic over which X_F has bad (multiplicative) reduction; V^good_mod its complement; fundamental groups Π_{X_F} ⊆ Π_{C_F}, Δ_X ⊆ Δ_C. Also F/F_mod is Galois of degree prime to l and the 2·3-torsion points of E_F are F-rational.

    Conclusion.

    Definition: no proof obligation.

    Obligations.

    • Field of moduli F_mod is not defined in Lean; the clauses mentioning it are stated relative to an arbitrary number field k.
    • C_F, Π_{X_F}, Π_{C_F}, Δ_X, Δ_C not statable.
    • Stable reduction is read as semistable reduction of E_F; this reading is recorded, not proved equivalent.

    Ambiguities.

    • "Stable reduction" of the punctured curve X_F is identified with semistable reduction of E_F; page 61 does not spell this out.
    • "Bad" is glossed on page 61 as multiplicative; the Lean statement uses multiplicative reduction of a minimal model.

    • IUT1.Def3_1.b.frag.semistable (fragment) IUT.PaperI.SemistableAtFinitePlaces statement formalized not extracted
      Record

      SemistableAtFinitePlaces W: at every height one prime v of the ring of integers of F, the minimal model of W over the valuation ring of v has good or multiplicative reduction.

      Explicit hypotheses.

      • F a number field; W an elliptic Weierstrass curve over F

      Conclusion.

IUT1.Ex3_2Example 3.2Frobenioids at Bad Nonarchimedean Primes
Record

Bad nonarchimedean v: the tempered Frobenioid F_v over the base D_v (category of connected tempered coverings of X_v); D^⊢_v = B(K_v)^0 as a full subcategory; the reciprocal theta function Θ_v normalized at √−1, defined up to 2l-th roots of unity; qq_v = q_v^{1/2l} (the q with a double underline, a chosen 2l-th root of the Tate parameter q_v, determined up to a μ_{2l}-multiple; page 71 rendered); split Frobenioids C^⊢_v and C^Θ_v with characteristic splittings; F^⊢_v and an isomorphism with the Θ-version.

Conclusion.

Construction of the local model data at bad v.

  • Frobenioids and tempered coverings.

IUT1.Cor3_7Corollary 3.7Θ-Links Between Θ-Hodge Theaters
Record

(i) The full poly-isomorphism †F^⊩_tht ⥲ ‡F^⊩_mod is nonempty; it is called the Θ-link. (ii) It induces composite full poly-isomorphisms †D^⊢_v ⥲ ‡D^⊢_v. (iii) It induces composite full poly-isomorphisms of unit groups O^× at each v.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • Two Θ-Hodge theaters relative to the same initial Θ-data.

Conclusion.

Nonemptiness of the full poly-isomorphism and the induced poly-isomorphisms on D^⊢ and O^×.

Obligations.

  • Proof: "follows immediately from the definitions"; nonemptiness rests on both collections being isomorphic to the same model.

Ambiguities.

  • Remark 3.7.1: no particular isomorphism in the Θ-link is distinguished.

IUT1.Def4_6Definition 4.6
Record

(i) D-NF-bridge: a poly-morphism D_J → D^⊚ from a capsule of D-prime-strips, isomorphic to the model by conjugation; morphisms are pairs (capsule-full poly-isomorphism, Aut_ε-orbit of isomorphisms) compatible with the arrows. (ii) D-Θ-bridge: a poly-morphism D_J → D_>, isomorphic to the model; morphisms are pairs (capsule-full poly-isomorphism, full poly-isomorphism). (iii) D-ΘNF-Hodge theater: a D-NF-bridge and a D-Θ-bridge sharing the capsule D_J, jointly isomorphic to the model; morphisms are compatible pairs inducing the same bijection of index sets.

Conclusion.

Definition: no proof obligation.

Ambiguities.

  • Composition of morphisms is described as evident.

IUT1.Prop4_7Proposition 4.7Transport of Label Classes of Cusps via Base-Bridges
Record

For a D-ΘNF-Hodge theater: (i) the D-Θ-bridge determines a bijection χ : J ⥲ F_l^; (ii) restriction via the j-th parts of the bridges gives isomorphisms of F_l^-torsors φ^LC_j, obtained from φ^LC_1 by the action of χ(j); (iii) there is a unique [ε] ∈ LabCusp(D^⊚) with φ^LC_j([ε]) ↦ χ(j), giving ζ : LabCusp(D^⊚) ⥲ J.

Conclusion.

Labels on the capsule and the element [ε].

IUT1.Prop4_8Proposition 4.8First Properties of Base-NF-Bridges, Base-Θ-Bridges, and Base-ΘNF-Hodge Theaters
Record

(i) Isomorphisms between two D-NF-bridges form an F_l^-torsor. (ii) Isomorphisms between two D-Θ-bridges, or two D-ΘNF-Hodge theaters, form a set of cardinality one. (iii) Gluings of a D-NF-bridge and a D-Θ-bridge into a D-ΘNF-Hodge theater form an F_l^-torsor. (iv) A functorial algorithm constructs, up to F_l^*-indeterminacy, a D-ΘNF-Hodge theater from a D-NF-bridge.

Conclusion.

Torsor and rigidity statements.

Obligations.

  • A formal proof needs the model objects; the torsor statements are combinatorial once the automorphism groups of the models are known.

IUT1.Cor5_3Corollary 5.3Isomorphisms of Global Frobenioids, Frobenioid-Prime-Strips, and Tempered Frobenioids
Record

(i) Isom of the global Frobenioids to Isom of their bases is bijective. (ii) Isom of F-prime-strips to Isom of the associated D-prime-strips is bijective. (iii) For F^⊢-prime-strips the map is surjective. (iv) Aut(F_v) → Aut(D_v) is bijective at bad v.

Conclusion.

Reconstruction of Frobenioid data from base data.

IUT1.Def5_5Definition 5.5
Record

(i) NF-bridge: (F_J, F^⊚, F^⊛, the restriction arrow, ψ^NF) where ψ^NF lifts a D-NF-bridge. (ii) Θ-bridge: (F_J, F_>, HT^Θ, ψ^Θ) with F_> tautologically associated to the Θ-Hodge theater and ψ^Θ determined by a D-Θ-bridge. (iii) ΘNF-Hodge theater: an NF-bridge and a Θ-bridge with a common capsule, lifting a D-ΘNF-Hodge theater. Morphisms are compatible collections of poly-isomorphisms.

Conclusion.

IUT1.Prop6_7Proposition 6.7Base-Θ-Bridges Associated to Base-Θ± -Bridges
Record

From a D-Θ^±-bridge, by passing to D_{T^}, identifying D_≻ with D_0, and replacing +-full poly-morphisms by full ones at good v and by those of Example 4.4 at bad v, a functorial algorithm produces a D-Θ-bridge D_{T^} → D_>, well defined up to unique isomorphism.

Conclusion.

Construction of a D-Θ-bridge from a D-Θ^±-bridge.

IUT2.ThmATheorem AFrobenius-pictures of Θ±ell NF-Hodge Theaters
Record

Abbreviated version of Corollary 4.10: for two Theta^{+-ell}NF-Hodge theaters it constructs the constant prime-strip F_triangle, the theta and Gaussian prime-strips F_env, F_gau with an evaluation isomorphism, the Theta^{x mu}- and Theta^{x mu}_gau-links as full poly-isomorphisms of F^{||- black-triangle x mu}-prime-strips, the coric F^{|- x mu}- and D^{|-}-prime-strips, and the Frobenius-pictures indexed by Z.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • initial Theta-data as in IUT I Definition 3.1
  • two Theta^{+-ell}NF-Hodge theaters relative to that data

Conclusion.

Same conclusions as Corollary 4.10 (i) to (iv) and (vi); the Introduction version omits Corollary 4.10 (v).

Obligations.

  • No Lean vocabulary exists for tempered fundamental groups, mono-theta environments, Frobenioids or Hodge theaters; the statement stays a catalog entry.
  • Proof is by reference to Corollary 4.10 (page 15 states Theorems A, B are abbreviated versions of Corollaries 4.10, 4.11).

IUT2.ThmBTheorem BÉtale-pictures of Base-Θ±ell NF-Hodge Theaters
Record

Abbreviated version of Corollary 4.11: the D-Theta^{+-ell}NF-links induced by either chain of Theorem A form a chain of full poly-isomorphisms of D^{|-}-prime-strips, so D^{|-}_triangle is a constant invariant (mono-analytic core); regarding each Hodge theater as a spoke gives an etale-picture admitting arbitrary permutation symmetries of the spokes.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • the situation of Theorem A (v)

Conclusion.

(i) constant invariant (-)D^{|-}_triangle; (ii) etale-picture with arbitrary permutation symmetries; (iii) compatibility with IUT I Theorem A (iii).

Obligations.

IUT2.Ex1_8Example 1.8Radial and Coric Data II: Concrete Examples
Record

Concrete multiradial environments, each defined by radial data, coric data and a radial algorithm whose radial functor is full and essentially surjective. (i) Radial data (Pi, G, alpha): Pi isomorphic to the tempered group Pi^tp of X_k (double underline), G isomorphic to G_k, alpha the full poly-isomorphism Pi/Delta -> G of topological groups; coric data a topological group isomorphic to G_k; radial algorithm (Pi, G, alpha) -> G (compare IUT I Fig. 3.2, Fig. 1.2). (ii) Using the algorithms (_TM) Pi -> (Pi acting on M_TM(Pi)) and (*_triangleright) G -> (G acting on O^triangleright(G)) and the tautological isomorphism (*_TM triangleright), radial data (Pi on M_TM(Pi), G on O^triangleright(G), alpha_triangleright) with alpha_triangleright the induced poly-isomorphism of MLF-Galois TM-pairs; radial algorithm to (G on O^triangleright(G)). (iii) For a closed subgroup Gamma of Zhat^x, the unit versions (*_x) with M^x_TM(Pi), O^x(G) and alpha_x the Gamma-orbit of the poly-isomorphism induced by alpha_triangleright; isomorphisms of coric data are Gamma-multiples. (iv) Torsion versions (*mu) and quotients M^{x mu} = M^x/M^mu, O^{x mu} = O^x/O^mu (*{x mu}); cyclotomic and co-cyclotomic versions of (iii); Ism(G), the compact group of G-isometries of O^{x mu}(G); closed subgroups Gamma^{x mu} of Ism(-) preserved by isomorphisms G_1 -> G_2 may replace Gamma (examples: Ism itself, and the image of Zhat^x -> Z_p^x in Ism). (v) Radial data (Pi on M^mu_TM(Pi), G on O^{x mu}(G), alpha_{mu, x mu}), with alpha_{mu, x mu} the poly-morphism determined by the full poly-isomorphism Pi/Delta -> G and the trivial homomorphism M^mu_TM(Pi) -> O^{x mu}(G); isomorphisms are Gamma^{x mu}-multiples; radial algorithm to (G on O^{x mu}(G)). (vi) Replacing M^mu_TM(Pi) by Pi_mu(M^Theta_(Pi)) tensor Q/Z gives an exterior-cyclotomic version of (v). (vii) Groupification algorithms (*_gp) and their profinite completions (*_gp-hat), with a Gamma-action and a poly-isomorphism alpha_gp-hat compatible with alpha_x. (viii) Radial data (Pi on M_TM(Pi), G on O^gp-hat(G), alpha_{triangleright, x mu}), a diagram of poly-morphisms through the groupifications and O^x(G) onto O^{x mu}(G); coric data as in (v) with Gamma^{x mu} = Im(Gamma); radial algorithm to (G on O^{x mu}(G)). (ix) The log-shell I(G) in O^{x mu}(G) (p^{-1} times the image of the G-invariants of O^x(G)); replacing G on O^{x mu}(G) by (G on O^{x mu}(G), I(G)) in (v), (vi), (viii) gives log-shell versions.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • k an MLF; Pi^tp of X_k (double underline) and G_k as in the setting of Section 1 of IUT II
  • (iii): Gamma a closed subgroup of Zhat^x
  • (iv), (v): Gamma^{x mu} a closed subgroup of Ism(-) preserved by arbitrary isomorphisms of topological groups

Conclusion.

Each of (i), (ii), (iii), (iv) (cyclotomic and co-cyclotomic versions), (v), (vi), (viii), (ix) defines radial data, coric data and a radial algorithm whose radial functor is full and essentially surjective, hence a multiradial environment; (vii) defines the groupification algorithms and the poly-isomorphism alpha_gp-hat used in (viii).

Obligations.

  • No Lean vocabulary exists for tempered fundamental groups, mono-theta environments, Frobenioids or Hodge theaters; the statement stays a catalog entry.

Ambiguities.

  • The decorations (hats, double underlines, bold mu) were read from the rendered pages 35 to 41 and are written in plain text here.

IUT2.Cor1_10Corollary 1.10Multiradial Mono-theta Cyclotomic Rigidity Isomorphisms
Record

For the exterior-cyclotomic multiradial environment of Example 1.8 (v), (vi), the cyclotomic rigidity isomorphism (^{mono-Theta}_Pi): (l . Delta_Theta)(Pi) -> Pi_mu(M^Theta_(Pi)), obtained from the mono-theta environment functorially attached to Pi, together with Pi and the two modules, defines a functor R -> F arising from a functorial algorithm in Pi; the natural functor Psi_R: R -> R^dagger is multiradially defined.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • (R, C, Phi) the radial environment (Pi acting on Pi_mu(M^Theta_*(Pi)) tensor Q/Z, G acting on O^{x mu}(G), alpha_{mu,x mu}) -> (G acting on O^{x mu}(G)) of Example 1.8 (v), (vi)

Implicit hypotheses.

  • Pi isomorphic to the tempered fundamental group of X_k as in Section 1 setup, page 20

Conclusion.

Psi_R is multiradially defined.

Obligations.

IUT2.Cor1_12Corollary 1.12Multiradial Constant Multiple Rigidity
Record

Multiradial constant multiple rigidity. With Gamma^{x mu} = Ism: (i) a functorial group-theoretic algorithm assigns to Pi the collection of pointed inversion automorphisms (iota, D); (ii) restriction to D gives splittings (dagger mu theta)(Pi) of M^{x mu}_TM(Pi) times (infinity theta(Pi)^iota / M^mu_TM(Pi)), the inverse image of the torsion being infinity theta(Pi)^iota, compatibly with the cyclotomic rigidity of Corollary 1.10; (iii) the data (mono-theta systems, the subsets dagger x theta, the splittings dagger mu theta, and the diagram from Pi_mu to O^{x mu}(G) through the trivial homomorphism) defines a multiradially defined functor.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • Gamma^{x mu} = Ism
  • environment of Example 1.8 (v), (vi)

Conclusion.

Psi_R: R -> R^dagger is multiradially defined.

Obligations.

  • No Lean vocabulary exists for tempered fundamental groups, mono-theta environments, Frobenioids or Hodge theaters; the statement stays a catalog entry.
  • The paper's proof of (iii) rests on the characteristic nature of the torsion submodules; Remark 1.12.2 (i), page 58, says the essential content is constructing theta(Pi) up to a mu_{2l}-indeterminacy via [EtTh] Theorem 1.10 (i).

IUT2.Def2_3Definition 2.3
Record

Notation at v in V^bad: tempered groups Pi_v, Pi^+-_v, Pi^cor_v and geometric subgroups, with Delta_v of index l in Delta^+-_v and Delta^+-_v of index 2l in Delta^cor_v; +-label classes of cusps LabCusp^+-(Pi), with an F_l^x action, zero element and +-canonical element; decomposition groups Pi_{v bullet t} for t in LabCusp^+-; a natural F_l^{x+-}-torsor structure and an outer isomorphism Pi_sup/Pi_sub -> F_l^{x+-}.

IUT2.Cor2_4Corollary 2.4F± l -Symmetric Two-torsion Translates of Cusps
Record

F_l^{x+-}-symmetric two-torsion translates of cusps: (i) for gamma, gamma' in Delta-hat^+-_v, a cuspidal inertia group I_t conjugated by gamma gamma' lies in the gamma'-conjugate of Pi_v if and only if it lies in the gamma'-conjugate of Pi^+-_v if and only if gamma is in Delta^+-_v; (ii) such an inclusion determines decomposition groups D_t, D_{mu_-}, D_{t,mu_-} (the latter an evaluation section at the mu_- translate of the cusp); (iii) for the bullet-t choice the construction is compatible with conjugation by Pi^cor_v, with Delta^cor/Delta^+- isomorphic to F_l^{x+-}.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • The notation of Definition 2.3 (v in V^bad); t in LabCusp^+-(Pi_v)

Conclusion.

Equivalence (a) <=> (b) <=> (c) and functorial construction of decomposition groups.

Obligations.

  • No Lean vocabulary exists for tempered fundamental groups, mono-theta environments, Frobenioids or Hodge theaters; the statement stays a catalog entry.
  • Proof of (c) => (a) uses IUT I Corollary 2.5 and Corollary 2.3 (v), (vi) (profinite versus tempered conjugates).

Ambiguities.

  • The opening hypothesis sentence was read from the text layer (prose only, no formula transcribed).

IUT2.Cor2_5Corollary 2.5Group-theoretic Theta Evaluation
Record

Group-theoretic theta evaluation: (i) (l . Delta_Theta) restricts isomorphically to the subgraph group Pi_{v black-triangle double-dot} and the quotient to G_v is reconstructible; (ii) restricting the iota-invariant theta classes to the subgraph and then to the evaluation sections D_{t,mu_-} yields mu_{2l}-orbits theta^t and mu-orbits infinity theta^t depending only on |t| in |F_l|; (iii) letting t vary gives a group-theoretic algorithm for the collections {theta^{|t|}}_{|t| in |F_l|}, functorial in Pi_v and compatible with independent Delta-hat^+-_v conjugacy actions.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • The notation of Corollary 2.4

Conclusion.

Existence of a functorial group-theoretic theta evaluation algorithm, valued in mu_{2l}-orbits.

Obligations.

  • No Lean vocabulary exists for tempered fundamental groups, mono-theta environments, Frobenioids or Hodge theaters; the statement stays a catalog entry.

IUT2.Rem2_5_1Remark 2.5.1
Record

(i) Relative to the standard cyclotomic rigidity isomorphism of Proposition 1.3 (ii) and the resulting Kummer map (delta of Corollary 2.5 (ii) taken to be the identity), for j in |F_l| the set theta^j(Pi_v) consists of precisely the mu_{2l}-orbit of the theta value qq_v^{j^2}, where j is the element of {0, 1, ..., l*} determined by j. (ii) The reciprocals of the l-th powers of these theta values (not the theta values themselves) correspond to the values Theta-ddot(+-sqrt(-1) . q_X^{j/2}) of the theta series Theta-ddot of [EtTh] Proposition 1.4, i.e. to values at points separated by periods from +-sqrt(-1); they have the property that Theta-ddot(c)/Theta-ddot(c') is a root of unity for c' obtained from c by an automorphism of Pi_v with c'/c a unit. (iii) The mu_{2l}-multiple indeterminacies for different j in |F_l| are independent, not synchronized; each arises from the action of (Delta^+-_v / Delta_v)^delta, of order 2l.

Conclusion.

theta^j(Pi_v) = mu_{2l} . qq_v^{j^2} (as orbits) for j = 0, ..., l*; the reciprocals of the l-th powers of these values correspond to Theta-ddot(+-sqrt(-1) . q_X^{j/2}); the mu_{2l}-indeterminacies for distinct j are independent.

Obligations.

  • Only the label bookkeeping is formalized, as a fragment; the identification of theta^j with the orbit of qq_v^{j^2} and part (ii) are not.

Ambiguities.

  • qq_v denotes the q with a double underline of IUT I, Example 3.2 (iv), page 71 (rendered): qq_v = q_v^{1/2l}, a chosen 2l-th root of the Tate parameter q_v, determined up to a mu_{2l}-multiple; relative to q_v the value qq_v^{j^2} is q_v^{j^2/(2l)}.
  • q_X in (ii) is the parameter of the theta series in the notation of [EtTh] Proposition 1.4 (page 73, rendered).

IUT2.Rem2_6_3Remark 2.6.3
Record

For a subgraph Gamma' of the line graph Gamma_Y (vertices Z), single-basepoint and splitting considerations force Gamma' connected and containing 0. For finite Gamma' define ||Gamma'|| = |Gamma'|^{-1} . sum over j in F_l* of min over integers j in (fiber of j) intersect Gamma' of j^2, where |Gamma'| is the cardinality of the image in F_l* of the nonzero vertices and the min over the empty set is 0. Claim (d): as a function of |Gamma'| this is a monotone increasing but not strictly increasing positive rational function that attains its maximum at |Gamma'| = l* and is constant for |Gamma'| >= l*.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • Gamma' a finite connected subgraph of Gamma_Y containing the vertex 0

Conclusion.

Maximizing ||Gamma'|| is achieved by Gamma'_Y-black-triangle (vertices 0, ..., l*), and the values qq_v^{j^2} are thereby distinguished.

Obligations.

  • Kernel-checked only for the elementary arithmetic: for Gamma' with vertex set [-a, b], |Gamma'| = min(max(a,b), l*) and ||Gamma'|| = (n+1)(2n+1)/6 with n = |Gamma'|, which is strictly increasing in n; hence ||Gamma'|| is monotone in max(a,b), maximal exactly when max(a,b) >= l*, with maximum l(l+1)/12. Infinite Gamma' (part (iii)) is not treated.

Ambiguities.

  • Page 79 defines |Gamma'| as the size of a subset of F_l*, so |Gamma'| <= l* always. Read literally, item (d) on page 80 then says ||Gamma'|| is a strictly increasing function of |Gamma'| on {1, ..., l*} (the Lean file proves this), which conflicts with 'not strictly increasing' and makes 'constant for |Gamma'| >= l*' vacuous beyond l*. The wording matches exactly if the variable is the radius max(a,b) of Gamma'; the Lean file proves both readings and adopts neither as the definition.
  • qq_v denotes the q with a double underline of IUT I, Example 3.2 (iv), page 71 (rendered): qq_v = q_v^{1/2l}, a chosen 2l-th root of the Tate parameter q_v, determined up to a mu_{2l}-multiple; relative to q_v the value qq_v^{j^2} is q_v^{j^2/(2l)}.
  • Page 79 defines ||Gamma'|| = |Gamma'|^{-1} . (sum); for Gamma' = {0} one has |Gamma'| = 0 and the page gives no value. The Lean function gammaNorm returns 0 there by division by zero; every Lean lemma that evaluates it assumes |Gamma'| >= 1.

Pages read: 79, 80 (both rendered)

IUT2.Cor2_9Corollary 2.9Theta Evaluation via Base-field-theoretic Cyclotomes

IUT2.Cor3_5Corollary 3.5Mono-theta-theoretic Gaussian Monoids
Record

Mono-theta-theoretic Gaussian monoids: (i) the outer F_l^{x+-}-action induces symmetrizing isomorphisms between the labeled pairs G_v acting on Psi_cns(M^Theta_*)_t, giving diagonal embeddings indexed by <|F_l|> and <F_l*>; the inner automorphism indeterminacy is independent of |t| (conjugate synchronization). (ii) a value-profile is an element of the product over |t| in F_l* of theta^{|t|}_env, a set of cardinality (2l)^{l*}; the Gaussian monoids are Psi_xi = Psi^x_cns <F_l*> . xi^N and infinity Psi_xi = Psi^x_cns <F_l*> . xi^{Q>=0}; restriction gives isomorphisms Psi_env -> Psi_gau well-defined up to a single inner automorphism; Psi_{2l . xi} is independent of xi. (iii) an isomorphism Psi_cns_0 -> Psi_cns_<F_l*> and splittings up to torsion of the Gaussian monoids.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • M^Theta_* a projective system of mono-theta environments as in Proposition 3.1

Conclusion.

Mono-theta-theoretic Gaussian monoids: (i) the outer F_l^{x+-}-action induces symmetrizing isomorphisms between the labeled pairs G_v acting on Psi_cns(M^Theta_*)_t, giving diagonal embeddings indexed by <|F_l|> and <F_l*>; the inner automorphism indeterminacy is independent of |t| (conjugate synchronization). IUT2.Rem3_6_2Remark 3.6.2
Record

On the diagram in the third display of Corollary 3.6 (ii), summarized as Fig. 3.1 (Frobenioid-theoretic theta monoids => group-theoretic theta monoids via Kummer, Galois evaluation down to group-theoretic Gaussian monoids, and 'forget' back to Frobenioid-theoretic Gaussian monoids). (i) The three portions correspond to Kummer, Galois evaluation and forgetting; forgetting yields post-anabelian monoids; the composite of Kummer and evaluation is a comparison isomorphism between Frobenius-like and etale-like structures, as in classical Hodge-Arakelov theory where the comparison isomorphism comes from evaluating theta functions and their derivatives at torsion points. (ii) Etale-like structures (etale or tempered fundamental groups) are invariant under non-ring/scheme-theoretic filters such as the Theta-link and the log-wall and are rigid via anabelian algorithms; Frobenius-like structures (abstract monoids) are not so rigidified, which allows such filters to be constructed; the two play complementary roles with respect to binding to conventional scheme theory (Fig. 3.2), and Kummer theory relates them within a Hodge theater. (iii) Composing the Theta-link qq_v -> Theta_v with the arrows of Fig. 3.1 gives the correspondence qq_v -> {qq_v^{j^2}}_{1<=j<=l*}, a 'modified Theta-link', viewed as an abstract combinatorial Frobenius lifting, a homotopy between the identity qq_v -> qq_v and qq_v -> qq_v^{(l*)^2}; the exponents {j^2} are highly distinguished (Remark 2.6.3). (iv) Grothendieck Conjecture-type results in anabelian geometry are comparison isomorphisms between polynomial-function-theoretic and group-theoretic collections of morphisms, obtained by combining considerations particular to the situation with Galois evaluation via Kummer theory of polynomial functions or differential forms at rational points.

Conclusion.

Interpretation of the diagram of Corollary 3.6 (ii): Kummer, Galois evaluation and forgetting as in Fig. 3.1; the complementary roles of etale-like and Frobenius-like structures; the modified Theta-link qq_v -> {qq_v^{j^2}}_{1<=j<=l*} as a homotopy between the identity and qq_v -> qq_v^{(l*)^2}; Grothendieck Conjecture-type results as comparison isomorphisms.

Obligations.

  • Interpretive; no mathematical claim beyond Corollary 3.6 and the cited results. The objects (Theta-link, Gaussian monoids) have no Lean vocabulary.

Ambiguities.

  • qq_v denotes the q with a double underline of IUT I, Example 3.2 (iv), page 71 (rendered): qq_v = q_v^{1/2l}, a chosen 2l-th root of the Tate parameter q_v, determined up to a mu_{2l}-multiple; relative to q_v the value qq_v^{j^2} is q_v^{j^2/(2l)}.

Pages read: 101, 102, 103, 104 (all rendered); IUT I page 71 (rendered) for qq_v

IUT2.Rem3_6_4Remark 3.6.4
Record

Explains why objects are known only up to isomorphism: Kummer N-th power morphisms respect multiplication but not addition, hence act as Frobenius-type morphisms between distinct ring structures, forcing the rigidity theory of [EtTh]. Galois evaluation is linear, so the first power of the theta function (and of its values) is the only power that crosses the mono-theta bridge; N-th powers for N > 1 are only defined as (-)^N of the first power, and other powers are not horizontal for the multiradial connection structure.

Conclusion.

Explains why objects are known only up to isomorphism: Kummer N-th power morphisms respect multiplication but not addition, hence act as Frobenius-type morphisms between distinct ring structures, forcing the rigidity theory of [EtTh]. Galois evaluation is linear, so the first power of the theta function (and of its values) is the only power that crosses the mono-theta bridge; N-th powers for N > 1 are only defined as (-)^N of the first power, and other powers are not horizontal for the multiradial connection structure.

Obligations.

  • Non-mathematical-claim content; nothing to formalize.

IUT2.Cor3_7Corollary 3.7Group-theoretic Gaussian Monoids and Uniradiality
Record

Group-theoretic Gaussian monoids and uniradiality: (i) each isomorphism M^Theta_*(Pi_v) -> M^Theta_*(dagger F_v) induces compatible isomorphisms from group-theoretic to post-anabelian Gaussian monoids and on units; (ii) the algorithms Pi_v -> Psi_gau depend on the cyclotomic rigidity of Corollary 1.11 (b), hence fail to be compatible with automorphisms of the pair G_v acting on Psi_{F_xi}^{x mu} coming from automorphisms of the pair with Psi^x: they only admit a uniradial formulation.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • The situation of Proposition 3.4: the full poly-isomorphism M^Theta_*(Pi_v) -> M^Theta_*(dagger F_v) of projective systems of mono-theta environments, with dagger F_v a tempered Frobenioid as in Proposition 3.3

Conclusion.

Gaussian monoid algorithm is uniradial as given.

Obligations.

  • No Lean vocabulary exists for tempered fundamental groups, mono-theta environments, Frobenioids or Hodge theaters; the statement stays a catalog entry.
  • The paper's proof reads: follows immediately from the definitions and the references quoted.

Ambiguities.

  • Remark 3.7.1 (page 112): a multiradial version is only obtained in IUT III via log-shells, with additional indeterminacies.
  • The opening hypothesis sentence was read from the text layer (prose only, no formula transcribed).

IUT2.Prop4_1Proposition 4.1Group-theoretic Gaussian Monoids at Good Nonarchimedean Primes
Record

At v in V^good nonarchimedean: (i) constant monoid Psi_cns(G_v) (isomorphic to O^triangleright_{F_v}) by a functorial group-theoretic algorithm; (ii) R>=0(G_v) with distinguished element log^{G_v}(p_v) and semi-simplification Psi^ss_cns = Psi^x_cns x R>=0; (iii) F_l^{x+-}-symmetrizing isomorphisms and diagonal submonoids; (iv) theta monoid Psi_env = Psi^x_cns x {R>=0 . log(p_v) . log(Theta)} (formal symbol) and Gaussian monoid Psi_gau = Psi^x_cns <F_l*> x {R>=0 . (..., j^2 . log(p_v), ...)} inside the product over j in F_l*, with j read as a natural number in {1, ..., l*}, and the formal evaluation isomorphism log(p_v) . log(Theta) -> (..., j^2 . log(p_v), ...).

Explicit hypotheses.

  • v in V^good intersect V^non

Conclusion.

Functorial group-theoretic construction of theta and Gaussian monoids and the formal evaluation isomorphism at good nonarchimedean v.

Obligations.

  • No Lean vocabulary exists for tempered fundamental groups, mono-theta environments, Frobenioids or Hodge theaters; the statement stays a catalog entry. Only the exponent vector (j^2) for j = 1, ..., l* is treated in Lean, as elementary arithmetic (its sum), which the paper does not state.

Ambiguities.

  • Remark 4.1.1 (i), page 122, calls this generalization tautological and largely vacuous.

IUT2.Cor4_5Corollary 4.5Group-theoretic Monoids Associated to Base-Θ±ell -Hodge Theaters
Record

Group-theoretic monoids for a D-Theta^{+-ell}-Hodge theater: (i) constant monoids Psi_cns(D) (up to Pi_v-conjugacy at nonarchimedean v); (ii) semi-simplifications with splittings and distinguished elements, and a global realified Frobenioid D^{||-}(D^{|-}) with Prime -> V and local isomorphisms rho; (iii) global F_l^{x+-}-symmetrizing isomorphisms, via the bijection zeta: LabCusp^+- -> T, compatible with the F_l^{x+-}-symmetry of the D-Theta^ell-bridge; (iv) local theta and Gaussian monoids with evaluation isomorphisms; (v) global realified theta and Gaussian Frobenioids D_env, D_gau (the latter a subcategory of the product over j in F_l* with vector of ratios (..., j^2 ., ...)) and a global formal evaluation isomorphism D_env -> D_gau.

  • double-dagger D a D-prime-strip

Conclusion.

Group-theoretic monoids for a D-Theta^{+-ell}-Hodge theater: (i) constant monoids Psi_cns(D) (up to Pi_v-conjugacy at nonarchimedean v); (ii) semi-simplifications with splittings and distinguished elements, and a global realified Frobenioid D^{||-}(D^{|-}) with Prime -> V and local isomorphisms rho; (iii) global F_l^{x+-}-symmetrizing isomorphisms, via the bijection zeta: LabCusp^+- -> T, compatible with the F_l^{x+-}-symmetry of the D-Theta^ell-bridge; (iv) local theta and Gaussian monoids with evaluation isomorphisms; (v) global realified theta and Gaussian Frobenioids D_env, D_gau (the latter a subcategory of the product over j in F_l* with vector of ratios (..., j^2 ., ...)) and a global formal evaluation isomorphism D_env -> D_gau.

IUT2.Rem4_5_4Remark 4.5.4
Record

D_gau is a weighted diagonal: its divisor (respectively rational function) monoid consists of (1^2 . phi, 2^2 . phi, ..., j^2 . phi, ...) (respectively with beta) for phi (beta) in the corresponding monoid of D^{||-}(D^{|-}).

Conclusion.

D_gau is a weighted diagonal: its divisor monoid consists of the tuples (1^2 . phi, 2^2 . phi, ..., j^2 . phi, ...) and its rational function monoid of (beta^{1^2}, ..., beta^{j^2}, ...) in multiplicative notation, for phi, beta in the corresponding monoids of D^{||-}(D^{|-}).

Obligations.

  • No Lean declaration states this remark. The sum of the weights j^2 is computed in the fragment IUT2.Prop4_1.frag.sum_exponents, which is elementary arithmetic not stated in the paper.

Frobenioid-theoretic monoids for a Theta^{+-ell}-Hodge theater: (i) Kummer isomorphisms Psi_cns(F) -> Psi_cns(D); (ii) semi-simplifications and a unique isomorphism C^{||-} -> D^{||-}(D^{|-}) compatible with Prime bijections and local isomorphisms; (iii) Kummer isomorphisms with t-independent conjugacy indeterminacy and F_l^{x+-}-symmetrizing isomorphisms; (iv) local theta and Gaussian monoids from a Theta-bridge glued via IUT I Proposition 6.7, with evaluation isomorphisms Psi_{F_env} -> Psi_env -> Psi_gau -> Psi_{F_gau}; (v) global realified Frobenioids C_env, C_gau and evaluation isomorphisms.

IUT2.Rem4_7_3Remark 4.7.3
Record

F_l^{x+-} versus F_l*-symmetry: F_l approximates Z; F_l^{x+-} corresponds to addition, involves the zero label and units, and is applied for conjugate synchronization (uniradial, but coric after mono-analyticization); F_l* corresponds to multiplication, separates the zero label, describes the Gaussian monoids as functions of j in F_l*, and is multiradial. Volumes at each j in F_l* are given weight 1/l* so that the diagonal constant distribution has weight 1.

Conclusion.

F_l^{x+-}-symmetry (additive, with the zero label, used for conjugate synchronization) and F_l*-symmetry (multiplicative, separating the zero label, used for the Gaussian monoids as functions of j) play different roles; the volume at each j in F_l* gets weight 1/l*, so the diagonal constant distribution has total weight 1.

Obligations.

  • The weighted average (1/l*) . sum j^2 is computed in a fragment; the paper does not state its value.

IUT2.Def4_9Definition 4.9
Record

(i) For a Frobenioid over the finite etale site of an MLF or CAF: monoids O^triangleright(A), mu_N(A), O^mu(A), O^x(A), O^{x mu_N}(A), O^{x mu}(A); a x-Kummer structure is a Zhat^x-orbit of isomorphisms O^x(G) -> O^x(A), a x mu-Kummer structure an Ism-orbit of isomorphisms O^{x mu}(G) -> O^{x mu}(A); x-Kummer structures are unique (Remark 1.11.1 (b)); a x mu-Kummer structure is equivalent to the lattices I_H. (ii) to (iv) at bad and good nonarchimedean w: build O^{black-triangle x mu}(A) = O^black-triangle(A) x O^{x mu}(A), a model split Frobenioid with x mu-Kummer structure, F^{black-triangle x mu}_w. (v) at archimedean w: inductive system of quotients by N-torsion with Kummer structure up to the nontrivial automorphism of the unit circle. (vi) the collections F^{black-triangle x mu}, F^x, F^{x mu} (isometry subcategories). (vii) F^{|-square}-prime-strips for |-square in {|-x, |-x mu, |-black-triangle x mu} (rendered page 158); morphisms are collections of isomorphisms. (viii) F^{||- black-triangle x mu}-prime-strip: data (C^{||-}, Prime -> V, F^{black-triangle x mu}, rho) as in IUT I Definition 5.2 (iv); the generators at bad v with the rho determine the pilot object (negative arithmetic degree).

Conclusion.

Definition (no proof obligation): fixes the x- and x mu-Kummer structures and the prime-strips on which the modified Theta-links act.

Obligations.

  • No Lean vocabulary exists for tempered fundamental groups, mono-theta environments, Frobenioids or Hodge theaters; the statement stays a catalog entry.

Ambiguities.

  • Rendered page 158 confirms 'v in V^bad (not equal to the empty set)' in (viii); the text layer drops the negation.

IUT2.Cor4_10Corollary 4.10Frobenius-pictures of Θ±ell NF-Hodge Theaters
Record

Frobenius-pictures. (i) constant prime-strip dagger F^{||-}_triangle with natural identification with dagger F^{||-}_mod (IUT I Definition 3.6 (c)), via the assignment '0, succ -> >' of Remark 3.8.2 (ii). (ii) theta and Gaussian prime-strips dagger F^{||-}_env, dagger F^{||-}_gau, identification dagger F^{||-}_env -> dagger F^{||-}_tht, and an evaluation isomorphism F_env -> F_gau. (iii) functoriality gives maps Isom_{F^{||-}}(F_env, ddagger F_triangle) -> Isom_{F^{||- black-triangle x mu}}(F^{x mu}_env, ddagger F^{x mu}_triangle), and the same with gau, between nonempty sets; the Theta^{x mu}-link is the full poly-isomorphism dagger F^{||- black-triangle x mu}_env -> ddagger F^{||- black-triangle x mu}_triangle and the Theta^{x mu}_gau-link the full poly-isomorphism dagger F^{||- black-triangle x mu}_gau -> ddagger F^{||- black-triangle x mu}_triangle. (iv) natural isomorphisms F^{|- x mu}_triangle -> F^{|- x mu}_env -> F^{|- x mu}_gau; composing with the links gives the full poly-isomorphism dagger F^{|- x mu}_triangle -> ddagger F^{|- x mu}_triangle (an invariant of both links), inducing the full poly-isomorphism of D^{|-}-prime-strips (the D-Theta^{+-ell}NF-link). (v) the induced isomorphism of global realified data (D^{||-}, Prime, rho) is compatible with the R>0-orbits of the isomorphisms from (C^{||-}_triangle, Prime, rho). (vi) chains indexed by Z form an oriented graph with translation symmetry but no arbitrary permutation symmetries.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • initial Theta-data
  • Theta^{+-ell}NF-Hodge theaters dagger HT, ddagger HT

Conclusion.

Frobenius-pictures. (i) constant prime-strip dagger F^{||-}_triangle with natural identification with dagger F^{||-}_mod (IUT I Definition 3.6 (c)), via the assignment '0, succ -> >' of Remark 3.8.2 (ii). (ii) theta and Gaussian prime-strips dagger F^{||-}_env, dagger F^{||-}_gau, identification dagger F^{||-}_env -> dagger F^{||-}_tht, and an evaluation isomorphism F_env -> F_gau. (iii) functoriality gives maps Isom_{F^{||-}}(F_env, ddagger F_triangle) -> Isom_{F^{||- black-triangle x mu}}(F^{x mu}_env, ddagger F^{x mu}_triangle), and the same with gau, between nonempty sets; the Theta^{x mu}-link is the full poly-isomorphism dagger F^{||- black-triangle x mu}_env -> ddagger F^{||- black-triangle x mu}_triangle and the Theta^{x mu}_gau-link the full poly-isomorphism dagger F^{||- black-triangle x mu}_gau -> ddagger F^{||- black-triangle x mu}_triangle. (iv) natural isomorphisms F^{|- x mu}_triangle -> F^{|- x mu}_env -> F^{|- x mu}_gau; composing with the links gives the full poly-isomorphism dagger F^{|- x mu}_triangle -> ddagger F^{|- x mu}_triangle (an invariant of both links), inducing the full poly-isomorphism of D^{|-}-prime-strips (the D-Theta^{+-ell}NF-link). (v) the induced isomorphism of global realified data (D^{||-}, Prime, rho) is compatible with the R>0-orbits of the isomorphisms from (C^{||-}_triangle, Prime, rho). (vi) chains indexed by Z form an oriented graph with translation symmetry but no arbitrary permutation symmetries.

Obligations.

IUT2.Rem4_10_1Remark 4.10.1
Record

The prime-strips F_env, F_gau are only well defined up to automorphisms at bad v of the split Frobenioids that induce the identity on the associated F^{|- x}-prime-strip; this indeterminacy is absorbed by the full poly-isomorphisms of Corollary 4.10 (iii).

Conclusion.

The prime-strips F_env, F_gau are only well defined up to automorphisms at bad v of the split Frobenioids that induce the identity on the associated F^{|- x}-prime-strip; this indeterminacy is absorbed by the full poly-isomorphisms of Corollary 4.10 (iii).

Obligations.

IUT2.Rem4_10_3Remark 4.10.3
Record

(i) coricity of F^{|- x mu} amounts to coricity of D^{|-} plus coricity of units modulo torsion; (ii) the value group portion is by no means preserved, it acts like a Frobenius morphism or a Teichmuller deformation; (iii) restricting the value group portion qq_v -> {qq_v^{j^2}}_{1<=j<=l*} to j = 1 gives a correspondence identifiable with the identity, but restriction to proper subsets of |F_l| is in general incompatible with the symmetries.

Conclusion.

(i) coricity of F^{|- x mu} amounts to coricity of D^{|-} plus coricity of units modulo torsion; (ii) the value group portion is by no means preserved, it acts like a Frobenius morphism or a Teichmuller deformation; (iii) restricting the value group portion qq_v -> {qq_v^{j^2}}_{1<=j<=l*} to j = 1 gives a correspondence identifiable with the identity, but restriction to proper subsets of |F_l| is in general incompatible with the symmetries.

Obligations.

IUT2.Rem4_11_1Remark 4.11.1
Record

The Theta^{x mu}_gau-link is roughly a transformation qq -> qq^{(1^2, ..., (l*)^2)} (qq the q with a double underline); the Frobenius-picture is an infinite iteration, the etale-picture a commutativity of these exponents, compared with cartesian versus polar coordinates for the Gaussian integral.

Conclusion.

The Theta^{x mu}_gau-link is roughly a transformation qq -> qq^{(1^2, ..., (l*)^2)} (qq the q with a double underline); the Frobenius-picture is an infinite iteration, the etale-picture a commutativity of these exponents, compared with cartesian versus polar coordinates for the Gaussian integral.

Obligations.

  • Nothing to formalize.

IUT3.Def1_1Definition 1.1
Record

Defines the tautological log-link from an F-prime-strip to its logarithm, the (full) log-link obtained by post-composing with a (full) poly-isomorphism, and log-shells: at nonarchimedean v the log-shell is (p_v^)^{-1} times the pre-log-shell, where p_v^ = p_v for odd p_v and p_v^2 for p_v = 2; at archimedean v a corresponding construction via the pointed universal covering. Mono-analytic versions for F^{⊢×μ}- and D^⊢-prime-strips are given in (iv), (v).

Explicit hypotheses.

  • initial Θ-data as in IUTchI Def 3.1
  • an F-prime-strip †𝔉

Conclusion.

Definitions only.

Obligations.

  • Formalize F-prime-strips and the full poly-isomorphism.
  • Formalize the log-shell as a compact submodule of the p_v-adic field and its archimedean analogue.

Ambiguities.

  • Not counted as extracted: the recorded conclusion ('Definitions only') is a label, not a statement of what is asserted. The content above is kept as a summary of the pages listed in source.

IUT3.Prop1_2Proposition 1.2log-links Between F-prime-strips
Record

Properties of log-links of F-prime-strips: (i) coricity of the associated D-prime-strips; (ii) simultaneous compatibility with ring structures of domain and codomain; (iii) log-volume compatibility; (iv) Kummer isomorphisms are not compatible with the log-link; (v) holomorphic log-shells are compact and contain the images of units and of the relevant invariants; (vi) to (viii) mono-analytic log-shells, with Ism-orbits at nonarchimedean v and {±1}-orbits on direct factors at archimedean v; (ix) coric holomorphic log-shells; (x) the Frobenius-picture.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • a log-link †𝔉 → ‡𝔉 of F-prime-strips

Conclusion.

Assertions (i) to (x).

Obligations.

  • Prove compactness of log-shells.
  • Prove log-volume compatibility of the log-link.
  • Model the failure of Kummer compatibility (iv) as a precise non-commutativity statement.

Ambiguities.

  • Not counted as extracted: the recorded conclusion ('Assertions (i) to (x)') is a label, not a statement of what is asserted; only part (x) has a part record. The content above is kept as a summary of the pages listed in source.

IUT3.Rem1_4_1Remark 1.4.1
Record

(i) The squares of the log-theta-lattice are far from 1-commutative, because vertical arrows depend on local ring structures and horizontal arrows are incompatible with them. (ii) Each execution of a horizontal arrow obligates a subsequent vertical arrow, because log-shells live on the codomain of a log-link.

Conclusion.

Interpretive remark.

Obligations.

  • State non-commutativity of a square precisely (what composites are compared, at which structural level).

Bi-cores of the Gaussian log-theta-lattice.

  • a Gaussian log-theta-lattice

Conclusion.

The conjunction of parts (i) to (v), as recorded in the part records: (i) Full poly-isomorphisms n,m HT^{D-Θ±ellNF} ≅ n,m+1 HT^{D-Θ±ellNF}; (ii) Full poly-isomorphisms along rows; (iii) F^{⊢×μ}△(n,m D^⊢△), up to a certain class of isomorphisms, is invariant under both kinds of arrows; (iv) Poly-isomorphisms of log-shell pairs I ⊆ log(−); (v) Isomorphisms of global realified Frobenioid data across the lattice.

Obligations.

  • See the part records.

IUT3.Rem1_5_4Remark 1.5.4
Record

Splits describing one arithmetic holomorphic structure (vertical line) from another into (a) passing from Frobenius-like to étale-like structures via Kummer isomorphisms and (b) transporting étale-like structures by multiradiality. Indeterminacies from (a) are called Kummer-detachment indeterminacies, those from (b) étale-transport indeterminacies. Examples: automorphisms of G_v not of scheme-theoretic origin (étale-transport); the non-commutative Frobenius-picture and the Ẑ^× indeterminacies of Remark 1.4.2 (Kummer-detachment).

Conclusion.

Terminology and interpretation.

Obligations.

  • Give a precise definition of each indeterminacy class as a set of maps or a group action.

Ambiguities.

IUT3.Prop3_1Proposition 3.1Local Holomorphic Tensor Packets
Record

Local holomorphic tensor packets. For an n-capsule {α𝔉}_{α∈A} of F-prime-strips and v | v_Q, log(αF_v) is an inductive limit of topological modules of finite dimension over Q_{v_Q}; the 1-tensor packet is v_Q ↦ log(αF_{v_Q}) = ⊕_{v|v_Q} log(αF_v), and the n-tensor packet is log(AF_{v_Q}) = ⊗{α∈A} log(αF{v_Q}) = ⊕_{{v_α}} {⊗α log(αF{v_α})}, tensor products of ind-topological modules (page 92). (i) The ind-topological field structures on the log(αF_v) determine an ind-topological ring structure on log(AF_{v_Q}), an inductive limit of direct sums of ind-topological fields; the decompositions are determined by the ring structure and compatible with the action of αΠ_v. (ii) For α ∈ A, v | v_Q: log(A,αF_v) = log(αF_v) ⊗ {⊗{β∈A∖{α}} log(βF{v_Q})} ⊆ log(AF_{v_Q}) is a direct summand and an inductive limit of direct sums of ind-topological fields. Tensoring with 1's in the factors β ≠ α gives a natural injective ring homomorphism log(αF_v) → log(A,αF_v) which, for suitable cofinal objects of the inductive systems, induces an isomorphism of an object of the domain onto each direct summand field of the corresponding object of the codomain. Hence the integral structure Ψ̄ = Ψ_{log(αF_v)} ∪ {0} ⊆ log(αF_v) determines integral structures on each direct summand field of log(A,αF_v) and log(AF_{v_Q}) (page 93). Remark 3.1.1 (i) (page 94): log(αF_v) ≅ k̄, Ψ̄ ≅ O_k̄, log(A,αF_v) ≅ ⊗ k̄ ≅ lim ⊕ k̄ ⊇ lim ⊕ O_k̄, so the integral structures are copies of O_k̄ in the copies of k̄.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • initial Θ-data as in IUTchI Def 3.1
  • an n-capsule {α𝔉}_{α∈A} of F-prime-strips (IUTchI §0, Def 5.2 (i))
  • v ∈ V, v_Q ∈ V_Q with v | v_Q (part (ii) also fixes α ∈ A)

Implicit hypotheses.

  • the notation of Def 1.1 (i), (ii), (iii)

Conclusion.

(i) log(AF_{v_Q}) carries an ind-topological ring structure, an inductive limit of direct sums of ind-topological fields, with decompositions determined by the ring structure and αΠ_v-compatible; (ii) log(A,αF_v) is a direct summand of the same kind, the map log(αF_v) → log(A,αF_v) is injective and induces isomorphisms onto each direct summand field for cofinal objects, and Ψ̄ determines, through these isomorphisms, an integral structure on each direct summand field.

Obligations.

  • Formalize topological tensor products of local fields and their decomposition into products of fields.
  • Record two lattices and the map between them. Lattice L1 (the one on page 93): the product, over the direct summand fields of a finite object of log(A,αF_v) or log(AF_{v_Q}), of the copy of the ring of integers transported from Ψ̄ by the isomorphism of page 93; this is the normalization (integral closure) in the product of fields. Lattice L2 (not on page 93): the image of the tensor product of the rings of integers of the factors. The map is the inclusion L2 → L1 induced by the inclusions of rings of integers. Assessment, not from the page: L2 can be a proper order in L1, so the two must not be identified. Every later use (log-volume 0 of the integral structures, Prop 3.9 (i)) refers to L1.
  • Prove that the integral structure transported to a summand field does not depend on the cofinal object chosen.

Ambiguities.

  • Assessment: the page does not mention a tensor product of rings of integers; an earlier description of these integral structures as tensor products of rings of integers was not what the page states and has been replaced.

Pages read: text; rendered and checked: pages 92, 93, 94

IUT3.Rem3_1_1Remark 3.1.1
Record

(i) Concrete description of Prop 3.1: log(αF_v) ≅ k̄ with k = K_v, Ψ̄ ≅ O_k̄; integral structures are copies of O_k̄ (pages 93 to 94). (ii) Weights: log-volumes on the portion of log(αF_v) for K_v are taken with weight [K_v : (F_mod)_v]^{-1}; for direct sums over v | v_Q the normalized weight is 1/([K_v:(F_mod)v]·Σ{w|v_Q}[(F_mod)w : Q{v_Q}]); for tensor products over α ∈ A the weight 1/∏α[K{v_α}:(F_mod)_{v_α}], normalized to 1/((∏α[K{v_α}:(F_mod)_{v_α}])·Σ_{{w_α}}∏_α[(F_mod)_{w_α}:Q_{v_Q}]); all normalized so that multiplication by p_{v_Q} adds or subtracts log(p_{v_Q}) (pages 94 to 95). (iii) Such weighted sums of log-volumes do not in general arise as a positive real multiple of the logarithm of a measure in the usual sense. For a direct product region (a product of compact subsets of positive measure of the direct summands) the weighted sum is computed directly; a direct product pre-region uses relatively compact factors; for arbitrary Borel sets the construction of (iv) applies, with V = ∏α V{v_Q}, N_v = ∏α [K{v_α}:(F_mod)_{v_α}], M_v the (radial, if archimedean) summand of Prop 3.1; on direct product regions a suitable positive normalization factor times the log of the E-weighted measure gives the weighted sums of (ii) (pages 95 to 96). (iv) For a nonempty finite set V, nonempty finite sets E_v and measure spaces (M_v, μ_v), the E-weighted measure μ_E(S) ∈ R_{≥0} ∪ {+∞} of a Borel set S ⊆ M_V is the measure of S_E = (∏{e∈E} S) ∩ M{E*V} in M_{E*V} ≅ M_W; it depends only on N = {N_v = |E_v|}; for S = ∏_v S_v with S_v compact of positive measure, (1/N_E)·μ^log_E(S) = Σ_v (1/N_v)·μ^log_v(S_v), N_E = ∏_v N_v (pages 96 to 97).

Explicit hypotheses.

  • the situation of Prop 3.1
  • for (iv): V a nonempty finite set, E_v nonempty finite sets, (M_v, μ_v) nonempty measure spaces in the sense of (iii)

Conclusion.

Weighted sums of log-volumes are defined with the weights of (ii); on direct product regions they equal (1/N_E)·log μ_E(S) = Σ_v (1/N_v)·log μ_v(S_v), and the E-weighted measure μ_E extends them to arbitrary Borel sets; they are in general not a positive multiple of the log of an ordinary measure on the original product.

Obligations.

  • Verify the (iv) identity as a product-measure computation on M_{E*V} ≅ M_W (elementary once the spaces are fixed).
  • Fix the normalization factor of (iii) that relates (1/N_E)·log μ_E to the weighted sums of (ii); the page says only 'a suitable positive real number'.

Ambiguities.

  • The value set of μ_E is R_{≥0} ∪ {+∞}; finiteness for the regions used later is not stated at this point.

IUT3.Prop3_5Proposition 3.5Kummer Theory and Upper Semi-compatibility for ±ell Vertically Coric Local LGP-Monoids
Record

(i) Vertically coric LGP-monoids and their Kummer isomorphisms. (ii) Upper semi-compatibility of the log-Kummer correspondence: (a) at nonarchimedean v_Q the mono-analytic integral structure contains the Kummer images of units, also after the m'-th iterate of the log-link; (b) at archimedean v_Q the closed unit ball contains the units and the closed balls of radius π, with a surjection via iterates; (c) at bad v the splitting monoids are mutually compatible up to roots of unity, which via the log-link is 'addition by zero'. Invariance under the column translation symmetry.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • a collection of distinct Θ^{±ell}NF-Hodge theaters {n,m HT}_{n,m∈Z} relative to the given initial Θ-data, arising from a Gaussian log-theta-lattice (Def 1.4)
  • for each n, the D-Θ^{±ell}NF-Hodge theater n,◦HT determined up to isomorphism by vertical coricity (Thm 1.5 (i))

Conclusion.

Upper semi-compatibility (containments) along each column.

Obligations.

  • State and prove the containments (a), (b) as inclusions of explicit subsets of p-adic and complex fields.
  • Prove that roots of unity in the domain map to 0 under the p-adic logarithm (c).

IUT3.Prop3_9Proposition 3.9Log-volume for Packets and Processions
Record

Log-volumes for packets and processions. Proof: 'follow immediately from the definitions'.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • the situations of Prop 3.2 (i), (ii) (parts (i), (ii)), of Prop 3.7 (i), (ii) (part (iii)), and an LGP-Gaussian log-theta-lattice (part (iv))

(i) packet-normalized and procession-normalized holomorphic log-volumes with the stated normalizations; (ii) compatible mono-analytic log-volumes, constructible from D^⊢-prime-strips; (iii) a global log-volume on families with zero local log-volume at all but finitely many v_Q, invariant under (M_MOD)_α and permutations of A, equal to the arithmetic degree relative to a suitable normalization; (iv) log-link compatibility of these log-volumes. Details in the part records.

Obligations.

  • See part records.

Parts, steps and fragments.

  • IUT3.Prop3_9.i (part or step) extracted extracted
    Record

    Packet-normalized log-volumes μ^log_{α,v_Q}, μ^log_{A,v_Q}, μ^log_{A,α,v} : M(I^Q(−)) → R, where M(−) is the set of nonempty compact open subsets (nonarchimedean) or compact closures of nonempty open subsets (archimedean). The local holomorphic integral structures have log-volume 0; multiplying by p_v adds −log(p_v) (nonarchimedean); multiplying by e adds 1 (archimedean). μ^log_{A,v_Q} is invariant under permutations of A. Procession-normalization: average over the capsules of a procession.

    Explicit hypotheses.

    • the situation of Prop 3.2 (i), (ii)
    • v ∈ V, v | v_Q, α ∈ A

    Conclusion.

    Log-volumes μ^log_{α,v_Q}, μ^log_{A,v_Q}, μ^log_{A,α,v} : M(I^Q(−)) → R exist, determined by the p_{v_Q}-adic (respectively radial) log-volumes on the direct summand fields and the normalized weights of Rem 3.1.1 (ii) to (iv); the integral structures of Prop 3.1 (ii) have log-volume 0; multiplication by p_v adds −log(p_v) (respectively by e adds 1); μ^log_{A,v_Q} is invariant under permutations of A; on products over the capsules of a procession (as in Prop 3.4 (ii)) the procession-normalized log-volume is the average over the capsules.

    Obligations.

    • Define the log-volume on each direct summand field as the logarithm of the Haar measure (p_{v_Q}-adic or radial) normalized so that the transported ring of integers has measure 1.
    • Define the packet log-volume as the weighted sum over direct summands with the weights of Rem 3.1.1 (ii) on direct product regions, and extend it to other elements of M(−) through the E-weighted measure of Rem 3.1.1 (iv). Do not define it as the logarithm of a single normalized Haar measure on the product: Rem 3.1.1 (iii), page 95, says such weighted sums do not in general arise as a positive multiple of the log of an ordinary measure; on direct product regions page 96 identifies them with a normalized log of μ_E on M_{E*V}, not of a measure on the original product.
    • Check the scaling rule against the weights: multiplication by p_v shifts each summand log-volume, and the normalized weights make the total shift −log(p_v).
    • Define the procession-normalized log-volume as the average over j ∈ F*_l (stated on pages 173 and 181), factor 1/l*; Prop 3.4 (ii) (page 102) and Rem 3.9.3 (page 120) corroborate the labels j ∈ {1, ..., l*}. Fix how the j = 0 packet of the procession enters.

    Ambiguities.

    • The extension of the weighted sum from direct product regions to all of M(−) is given only through Rem 3.1.1 (iv) with 'a suitable normalization factor' (page 96); the factor is not displayed.

    Pages read: text; rendered and checked: pages 115, 116; rendered and checked: pages 95, 96, 97, 102, 115, 116

IUT3.Rem3_9_3Remark 3.9.3
Record

Weights in the procession-normalization must be equal: for j' ≤ j1, j2 the same log-shell labeled j' occurs in capsules S^±{j1+1} and S^±{j2+1}, and compatibility with the inclusion morphisms of the procession forces equal weights.

Conclusion.

Only plain averages are compatible with the procession inclusions.

Obligations.

  • State precisely what compatibility with inclusion morphisms means for a weighted average, then prove that it forces equal weights.

Ambiguities.

  • The argument does not specify which quantity must be preserved under inclusions; the conclusion 'no choice but to assign the same weights' depends on that specification.

  • IUT3.Rem3_9_5.i (part or step) extracted blocked by missing justification
    Record

    For a subset U of I^Q(−) that contains a relatively compact subset of finite log-volume: if U is relatively compact, its holomorphic hull is the smallest subset λ·O (λ with every component nonzero in the direct sum decomposition) containing U; otherwise the hull is all of I^Q(−). 'One verifies immediately that the holomorphic hull is well-defined.'

    Explicit hypotheses.

    • the situation of Prop 3.9 (i)
    • a subset U of I^Q(αF_{v_Q}) (respectively I^Q(AF_{v_Q}), I^Q(A,αF_v)) containing a relatively compact subset of finite log-volume (> −∞)

    Conclusion.

    The holomorphic hull of a relatively compact U is the smallest subset λ·O (λ with every component nonzero) containing U; the hull of a U that is not relatively compact is all of I^Q(−); 'one verifies immediately' that the hull is well defined.

    Obligations.

    • Prove existence and uniqueness of a smallest λ·O containing a relatively compact U (componentwise this is the smallest scaled ring of integers containing the projection, which exists by discreteness of the value group).
    • Prove the hull is independent of the choice of λ among those giving the same set.

    Ambiguities.

    • At archimedean components the ring O is a closed unit ball in C; 'smallest λ·O' then refers to the smallest closed ball, which exists by compactness; the text does not separate the cases.

    Pages read: text; rendered and checked: page 127; rendered and checked: page 127

  • IUT3.Rem3_9_5.ii (part or step) extracted extracted
    Record

    ℙ = direct product regions in I^Q(−) (Rem 3.1.1 (iii)); ℍ = hulls H ⊊ I^Q(−); ℍ ⊆ ℙ. Forming the hull gives φ : ℙ → ℍ, 'characterized uniquely' by (P1) φ(H) = H for H ∈ ℍ, (P2) P ⊆ φ(P), (P3) P₁ ⊆ P₂ ⇒ φ(P₁) ⊆ φ(P₂). Since 'as is easily verified' any intersection of elements of ℍ which is of finite log-volume (the qualifier applies to the intersection) determines an element of ℍ, it 'follows formally' that φ(P) = ⋂_{ℍ∋H⊇P} H. φ is described as an adjoint of ℍ ⊆ ℙ, a canonical splitting, an integration operation (page 127).

    Explicit hypotheses.

    • the situation of Rem 3.9.5 (i)

    Conclusion.

    φ is the unique map ℙ → ℍ with (P1), (P2), (P3), and φ(P) = ⋂_{ℍ∋H⊇P} H for every P ∈ ℙ.

    Obligations.

    • Prove the closure claim for actual hulls: an intersection of hulls which has finite log-volume is a hull ('as is easily verified', page 127).
    • Prove that the hull map of Rem 3.9.5 (i) satisfies (P1), (P2), (P3) and maps ℙ into ℍ ('essentially a tautology', page 127).

    Ambiguities.

    • Assessment: the order-theoretic fragments show the formula follows from (P1), (P2), (P3), ℍ ⊆ ℙ and φ(ℙ) ⊆ ℍ without the closure claim; the closure claim is needed only for existence. The text presents it as the reason for the formula.

    Pages read: text; rendered and checked: page 127; rendered and checked: page 127

IUT3.Thm3_11Theorem 3.11Multiradial Algorithms via LGP-Monoids/Frobenioids
Record

Multiradial algorithms via LGP-monoids and Frobenioids, for an LGP-Gaussian log-theta-lattice. Three parts: (i) multiradial representation, (ii) log-Kummer correspondence, (iii) Θ^{×μ}_LGP-link compatibility. Proof: the assertions 'follow immediately from the definitions and the references quoted'. Not stated as a Lean Prop: the vocabulary does not exist.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • initial Θ-data (F̄/F, X_F, l, C_K, V, V^bad_mod, ε) as in IUTchI Def 3.1
  • a collection of distinct Θ^{±ell}NF-Hodge theaters {n,m HT}_{n,m ∈ Z} arising from an LGP-Gaussian log-theta-lattice

Implicit hypotheses.

  • the D-Θ^{±ell}NF-Hodge theater n,◦HT is determined up to isomorphism by vertical coricity (Thm 1.5 (i), Rem 3.8.2)

Conclusion.

(i) the data (a), (b), (c), regarded up to (Ind1) and (Ind2), are constructible by an algorithm in the procession, functorial in isomorphisms of processions, and the étale-picture permutation symmetries induce compatible poly-isomorphisms; (ii) Kummer isomorphisms relate these coric data to the data of each n,m HT, mutually compatible along columns for (b) and the MOD part of (c), upper semi-compatible for (a) (Ind3), and compatible with log-volumes; (iii) these constructions are compatible with the horizontal arrows, for (iii)(c), (d) up to (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3).

Obligations.

  • See part records; docs/THEOREM_3_11.md separates each claim.

IUT3.Rem3_11_1Remark 3.11.1
Record

Interpretation of Thm 3.11: (ii) the q-pilot could be replaced by q^λ; the codomain is coric. (iii) definitions of IPL (input prime-strip link) and SHE (simultaneous holomorphic expressibility); SHE corresponds to a closed loop of the subquotient operations of Rem 3.9.5. (iv) APT and HIS. (v) input/output summary. (vi) the naive tautological algorithm 'λ-th power of q' has SHE at the cost of sacrificing the analogue of IPL (page 164). (vii) distinct labels: IPL holds, while for SHE the diagram reads '??' (it is 'by no means clear' how to establish it); forced identification: SHE holds, while for IPL the diagram reads '??'; considering both assignments simultaneously and consistently makes q well defined only up to q^{λ^n}, n ∈ Z, a trivial multiradial representation {q^{λ^n}}_{n∈Z} that satisfies the formal analogues of both IPL and SHE (pages 164 to 165). (viii) the trivial representation is a toy model for IPL and SHE but useless for the log-volume estimates of Cor 3.12 (page 165).

Conclusion.

Definitions of the properties IPL, SHE, APT, HIS used in Cor 3.12 Step (xi).

Obligations.

  • Give IPL and SHE as precise properties of an algorithm (input type, output type, the relation to be preserved).

Ambiguities.

  • IPL and SHE are stated informally; their formal content (which map, which structure) is the main missing vocabulary for Cor 3.12.

Pages read: text; rendered and checked: pages 164, 165
