IUT3.Cor3_12Corollary 3.12Log-volume Estimates for Θ-Pilot Objects
Record

Log-volume estimates for Θ-pilot objects. In the situation of Thm 3.11, with −|log(Θ)| ∈ R ∪ {+∞} and −|log(q)| ∈ R as defined in the statement (IUT3.Cor3_12.Statement), −|log(Θ)| ∈ R and −|log(Θ)| ≥ −|log(q)|. Proof: an opening reduction and Steps (i) to (xii). Not stated as a Lean Prop. Lean lemmas about the two numbers alone are recorded as fragments of IUT3.Cor3_12.Statement, IUT3.Cor3_12.Reduction and IUT3.Cor3_12.StepXI.f; they do not formalize this corollary.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • the situation of Thm 3.11

Conclusion.

Two conjuncts: (F) −|log(Θ)| ∈ R, and (I) −|log(Θ)| ≥ −|log(q)|. The 'i.e.' clause of page 174 (C_Θ ≥ −1 for every real C_Θ with −|log(Θ)| ≤ C_Θ·|log(q)|) reformulates (I) only: it holds vacuously when −|log(Θ)| = +∞ (IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.constantForm_of_top). The statement is therefore (F) ∧ (I), equivalently (F) ∧ (the 'i.e.' clause); finiteness is kept as a separate conjunct in both formulations.

Obligations.

  • See the step records and docs/COR312_ANALYSIS.md.
  • Prove (F) separately: compactness of the 1,◦U_{j,v_Q} (asserted '[easily verified]' on page 175) and the all-but-finitely-many support condition of the global log-volume (Prop 3.9 (iii), page 117).

Pages read: text; rendered and checked: pages 173, 174, 175, 181, 182, 183, 184, 185, 186

Parts, steps and fragments.

  • IUT3.Cor3_12.Reduction (part or step) extracted blocked by missing justification
    Record

    Since |log(q)| > 0 one may assume −|log(Θ)| < 0 whenever it is real. U = {n,◦U_{j,v_Q}} for j ∈ |F_l|, v_Q ∈ V_Q: unions of possible images in n,◦U^Q_{j,v_Q} = I^Q(S^±{j+1}; n,◦D^⊢{v_Q}). Ū = their holomorphic hulls relative to the holomorphic structure labeled n,◦. From the '[easily verified] compactness' of the 1,◦U_{j,v_Q} and the definition of log-volume, −|log(Θ)| is finite, hence negative. One may restrict to possible images that can be interpreted as an F^{⊩▶}-prime-strip.

    Explicit hypotheses.

    • inherits the hypotheses of IUT3.Cor3_12.Statement

    Conclusion.

    Without loss of generality −|log(Θ)| < 0 whenever −|log(Θ)| ∈ R (a nonnegative value already satisfies (I)); −|log(Θ)| is finite, hence negative, by the compactness of the 1,◦U_{j,v_Q} and the definition of the log-volume; one may restrict to possible images interpretable as an F^{⊩▶}-prime-strip.

    Obligations.

    • The numerical reduction concerns the inequality conjunct only; finiteness is a separate claim.
    • Prove compactness of each 1,◦U_{j,v_Q} (union over the orbit of the action generated by (Ind1) and (Ind2), enlarged by (Ind3)); page 175 calls it '[easily verified]' and gives no argument. Requires the action to factor through a compact or finite group and the (Ind3) enlargements to be bounded. A direct product Ind1 × Ind2 is not assumed: page 154 does not state that the actions commute.
    • Prove that compactness implies each local hull is a proper λ·O, hence of finite local log-volume (Rem 3.9.5 (i)).
    • Prove the support condition: the local log-volumes of the hulls vanish for all but finitely many v_Q (Prop 3.9 (iii), page 117), so that the global average is a real number; placewise compactness alone does not give this.
    • Justify that restricting to possible images interpretable as F^{⊩▶}-prime-strips does not change the hull or only shrinks it in a way consistent with the inequality.

    Ambiguities.

    • The WLOG reduction is numerical (fragment IUT3.Cor3_12.Reduction.frag.inequality_of_nonneg). The finiteness claim rests on '[easily verified] compactness' (page 175), for which no argument is given.
    • The restriction to images interpretable as an F^{⊩▶}-prime-strip shrinks the union; the effect on the hull and hence on −|log(Θ)| is not discussed.

    Pages read: text; rendered and checked: pages 174, 175

    Parts, steps and fragments.

    • IUT3.Cor3_12.Reduction.frag.inequality_of_nonneg (fragment) IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.inequality_of_nonneg statement formalized conditionally verified
      Record

      For given numbers, 0 ≤ negLogTheta implies −absLogQ ≤ negLogTheta.

      Explicit hypotheses.

      • v : PilotLogVolumes, a structure of two given numbers: negLogTheta : EReal with negLogTheta ≠ ⊥, and absLogQ : ℝ with 0 < absLogQ
      • h : 0 ≤ negLogTheta

      Conclusion.

      ((−absLogQ : ℝ) : EReal) ≤ negLogTheta

      Covers.

      the first sentence of the proof (the reduction to −|log(Θ)| < 0), page 174

      Not covered.

      Finiteness of −|log(Θ)| (page 175, compactness), the restriction to images interpretable as F^{⊩▶}-prime-strips, and all objects.

      Obligations.

      • The field 0 < absLogQ stands for '|log(q)| > 0 is easily computed' (page 174), not proved in this project.

      Pages read: rendered and checked: page 174

  • IUT3.Cor3_12.Statement (part or step) interpretation ambiguous blocked by missing justification
    Record

    −|log(Θ)| ∈ R ∪ {+∞} is the procession-normalized mono-analytic log-volume (average over j ∈ F*_l) of the holomorphic hull of the union of the possible images of a Θ-pilot object, relative to the Kummer isomorphisms of Thm 3.11 (ii), in the multiradial representation of Thm 3.11 (i), subject to Ind1, Ind2, Ind3. −|log(q)| ∈ R is the procession-normalized mono-analytic log-volume of the image of a q-pilot object, relative to the Kummer isomorphisms, in the multiradial representation, not subject to the indeterminacies; △ is the result of identifying the labels 0 and ⟨F*_l⟩. |log(q)| > 0 is 'easily computed' from the q-parameters at v ∈ V^bad (≠ ∅).

    Explicit hypotheses.

    • situation of Thm 3.11

    Implicit hypotheses.

    • V^bad ≠ ∅ (IUTchI Def 3.1 (b))
    • the union of possible images is a subset of the packets on which the hull is defined (Rem 3.9.5 (i) requires a relatively compact subset of finite log-volume)

    Conclusion.

    (F) −|log(Θ)| ∈ R, and (I) −|log(Θ)| ≥ −|log(q)|; the 'i.e.' clause on page 174 is equivalent to (I) only under (F).

    Obligations.

    • Define the set of possible images: the orbit of the image under the action generated by (Ind1) and (Ind2), together with the containments allowed by (Ind3).
    • Define the hull of the union and its log-volume.
    • Prove |log(q)| > 0 from ord_v(q_v) > 0 at bad v and V^bad ≠ ∅.
    • Prove finiteness (F) as its own claim: the compactness of each 1,◦U_{j,v_Q} asserted on page 175, and the support condition of Prop 3.9 (iii) (page 117) for the family of hulls.
    • The comparison is with the hull of the union of the possible images: a single image whose hull dominates the q-pilot region is a sufficient route, not a necessary one (two singleton images of measure 1/4 each jointly have log-volume log(1/2), while each has log(1/4)).

    Ambiguities.

    • Averaging index: the statement (page 173) and Step (x) (page 181) say explicitly that the average is over j ∈ F*_l, so the factor is 1/l* (not displayed); Prop 3.4 (ii) (page 102) and Rem 3.9.3 (page 120) corroborate the labels j ∈ {1, ..., l*}. Pages 153 and 174 index the packets and the sets U, Ū by j ∈ |F_l|; they state no average. Page 183 speaks of a log-volume 'normalized, with respect to j ∈ |F_l|', with no factor; an average over |F_l| would carry 1/(l*+1). How the j = 0 component of a region indexed over |F_l| enters the average over F*_l (dropped, or identified with the diagonal label ⟨F*_l⟩ through △, pages 174 and 181) is not stated.
    • The extracted text of page 174 reads 'V^bad (= ∅)'; the rendered page reads '(≠ ∅)'. This is an extraction artifact, not a discrepancy in the paper.
    • 'possible images' and 'subject to the indeterminacies' are not defined as a set by an explicit formula; whether Ind3 enlarges each image to a containing region or acts as a separate indeterminacy is not stated.

    Pages read: text; rendered and checked: pages 173, 174

      logVolume μ S := ENNReal.log (μ S) for a measure μ on a measurable space X and a set S (value −∞ when μ S = 0, +∞ when μ S = ∞), with lemmas: logVolume_mono (S ⊆ T ⇒ logVolume μ S ≤ logVolume μ T); logVolume_eq_bot_iff (= ⊥ ↔ μ S = 0); logVolume_eq_top_iff (= ⊤ ↔ μ S = ⊤); logVolume_finite_iff (≠ ⊥ ∧ ≠ ⊤ ↔ μ S ≠ 0 ∧ μ S ≠ ⊤); logVolume_neg_iff (< 0 ↔ μ S < 1).

      Explicit hypotheses.

      • μ : Measure X
      • S, T : Set X (and h : S ⊆ T for logVolume_mono)

      Conclusion.

      Definition and the five stated properties of the log-volume of an arbitrary measure.

      Covers.

      'log-volume' as the natural logarithm of a measure (Prop 3.9 (i), pages 115 to 116; Rem 3.1.1 (iv), page 97, for the convention 'log' of a measure)

      Not covered.

      The normalizations of Prop 3.9 (i) (integral structures of log-volume 0, the p_v and e scaling rules) and the weighted sums of Rem 3.1.1; the measure is arbitrary. Also records logVolume_mono, logVolume_eq_bot_iff, logVolume_eq_top_iff, logVolume_finite_iff, logVolume_neg_iff under this one record.

  • IUT3.Cor3_12.StepIII (part or step) extracted extracted
    Record

    Unit and value group portions on the 0-column must be related simultaneously to those on the 1-column; relating them via two different links (0,m) → (1,m) and (0,m') → (1,m'), m' ≠ m, is made practically impossible by the non-commutativity of the lattice (Rem 3.11.3 (i), (ii)). Hence a single Θ^{×μ}_LGP-link.

    Explicit hypotheses.

    • inherits the hypotheses of IUT3.Cor3_12.Statement

    Conclusion.

    Unit and value group portions on the 0-column must be related to those on the 1-column simultaneously, through one Θ^{×μ}_LGP-link; two links at different heights would make this practically impossible. Justification in the text: assertion with interpretive argument.

    Obligations.

    • None formal: this step justifies a choice of setting rather than a claim.

    Pages read: text; text: pages 175 to 186

  • IUT3.Cor3_12.StepIV (part or step) extracted extracted
    Record

    To apply bi-coricity of units, one must use units vertically once-shifted, at (n,m−1), relative to the value group structures at (n,m) (Rem 3.11.3 (ii)). The conflict between needing a vertical shift and the impossibility of shifting (Step iii) is resolved by working on the 0-column with structures invariant under vertical shifts, namely vertically coric structures (n,◦), related to Frobenius-like structures by the log-Kummer correspondence of Thm 3.11 (ii), at the cost of the upper semi-compatibility Ind3.

    Explicit hypotheses.

    • inherits the hypotheses of IUT3.Cor3_12.Statement

    Conclusion.

    The conflict between the vertical shift needed for bi-coricity of units and the impossibility of vertical shifts is resolved by working on the 0-column with vertically coric structures related to Frobenius-like ones by the log-Kummer correspondence, at the cost of (Ind3). Justification in the text: argument by reference.

    Obligations.

    • Formalize the passage from Frobenius-like data at (0,0) to vertically coric data at (0,◦) and quantify the loss as a containment.

  • IUT3.Cor3_12.StepX (part or step) extracted blocked by missing justification
    Record

    Kummer-detachment gives Ind1 (permutation symmetries of the étale picture), Ind2 (automorphisms of the horizontal arrows) and Ind3 (vertical arrows). The procession-normalized log-volumes (average over j ∈ F*_l) are invariant under Ind1, Ind2 '[one verifies immediately, cf. Prop 3.9 (ii)]' and convert Ind3 into an inequality from above. log-link compatibility (Prop 3.9 (iv)) makes these log-volumes compatible with the arrows of the log-Kummer correspondence. Tensor products identify multiplication by integers, hence its effect on log-volumes, at different labels j ∈ F*_l.

    Explicit hypotheses.

    • inherits the hypotheses of IUT3.Cor3_12.Statement

    Conclusion.

    Kummer-detachment relates the Frobenius-like structures at (0,0) to the multiradial representation at the cost of (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3); the procession-normalized log-volumes are invariant under (Ind1), (Ind2) and convert (Ind3) into an inequality from above, and are compatible with the arrows of the log-Kummer correspondence. Justification in the text: assertion with citation.

    Obligations.

    • Prove invariance of the averaged log-volume under each element of Ind1 (procession automorphisms) and each element of Ind2 (∏ Ism or ±1 on direct summands).
    • Prove that Ind3 containments give log-volume upper bounds (monotonicity of measure).
    • Prove that invariance holds for the action generated by (Ind1) and (Ind2), not only for each separately; do not assume the two actions commute.

    Ambiguities.

    • Ind1 is defined (Thm 3.11 (i)) as automorphisms of the procession; Step (x) describes it as arising from permutation symmetries of the étale picture. The two descriptions are not shown to coincide.
    • The average is over j ∈ F*_l here (page 181) and on page 173, stated explicitly, factor 1/l*, corroborated by Prop 3.4 (ii) and Rem 3.9.3 (pages 102, 120); page 183 speaks of a normalization 'with respect to j ∈ |F_l|' without a factor; the treatment of the label 0 component is not stated.

    Pages read: text; rendered and checked: page 181

    • IUT3.Cor3_12.StepXI.b (part or step) interpretation ambiguous blocked by missing justification
      Record

      The multiradial construction algorithm of Thm 3.11 yields a collection of possibilities of output data in 0,◦U ≅ 1,◦U (the isomorphism from the permutation symmetries of Thm 3.11 (i)), satisfying IPL and SHE (Rem 3.11.1). The F^{⊩▶} portion of the link to the input data consists of full poly-isomorphisms of F^{⊩▶}-prime-strips; the F^{⊢×μ} portion is 'somewhat more complicated' because of Ind1, Ind2, Ind3. The algorithm is treated as 'some' algorithm with properties IPL and SHE, temporarily forgetting its internal structure (HIS).

      Conclusion.

      Nature of the justification in the text: assertion with citation.

      Obligations.

      • State IPL as a precise property: each possible output is related to the input F^{⊩▶×μ}-prime-strip by an isomorphism of F^{⊩▶}-prime-strips.
      • State SHE precisely: the output is expressible in the arithmetic holomorphic structure of the 1-column.
      • Prove that the algorithm of Thm 3.11 satisfies both, jointly.

      Ambiguities.

      • The precise relation 'somewhat more complicated' for the F^{⊢×μ} portion is not specified.

      Pages read: text; rendered and checked: pages 181, 182

    • IUT3.Cor3_12.StepXI.d (part or step) interpretation ambiguous extracted
      Record

      The hull outputs are localizations of arithmetic vector bundles of rank > 1, while the q-pilot object is an arithmetic line bundle. Forming a suitable positive tensor power of the determinant (Rem 3.9.5 (vii), (Ob3), (Ob4)) and applying the log-volume '[suitably normalized, with respect to j ∈ |F_l|]' to the region 1,◦Ū and to the region of the q-pilot object at (1,0) on 1,◦U^Q gives completely comparable objects R_{≤−|log(Θ)|} = {λ ∈ R | λ ≤ −|log(Θ)|} ⊆ R and −|log(q)| ∈ R, where −|log(Θ)| is by definition the (negative) log-volume of 1,◦Ū and −|log(q)| the log-volume of the q-pilot region. Global arithmetic degrees are log-volumes (Prop 3.9 (iii)). The log-Kummer correspondence in the 1-column rectifies the vertical shift between the unit portion of 1,0 𝔉_△^{⊩▶×μ} and the log-shells forming 1,◦U.

      Conclusion.

      Nature of the justification in the text: argument with citation.

      Obligations.

      • Prove, for hulls λ·O (the class for which pages 132 and 133 state it, with details left to the reader), that the degree of det_{⊗M}(H), formed with the weights of Rem 3.1.1 (iv) and tensored with the inverse determinant of the structure sheaf (Ob3-1-2), equals the normalized log-volume of H times an explicit constant, the same constant being used for the q-pilot region.
      • Prove that the q-pilot region and the hull are measured by the same normalized log-volume (same index set for the average, same weights).
      • Prove that the log-Kummer correspondence identifies the unit portion at (1,0) with the log-shells at (1,◦) without changing log-volumes.

      Ambiguities.

      • Normalization 'with respect to j ∈ |F_l|' (page 183, no factor displayed) versus the average over j ∈ F*_l of pages 173 and 181 (stated explicitly, factor 1/l*); the rule for the j = 0 component is not stated.
      • The set R_{≤−|log(Θ)|} is introduced as 'possible log-volumes of pilot-object output data'; why every real number below −|log(Θ)| is a possible log-volume, rather than only the log-volumes of actual possible images, is not argued.

      Pages read: text; rendered and checked: page 183; rendered and checked: pages 102, 116, 131, 132, 133, 183

    • IUT3.Cor3_12.StepXI.f (part or step) interpretation ambiguous blocked by missing justification
      Record

      The construction of R_{≤−|log(Θ)|} is subject to the condition that it constitutes a construction (perhaps only up to some sort of 'approximation', due to the indeterminacies) of the pilot-object log-volume −|log(q)| of the input data. The inclusion −|log(q)| ∈ R_{≤−|log(Θ)|}, hence −|log(q)| ≤ −|log(Θ)|, then follows formally.

      Conclusion.

      Nature of the justification in the text: assertion followed by a formal step.

      Obligations.

      • Prove the inclusion −|log(q)| ∈ R_{≤−|log(Θ)|} from properties of the construction (this is the whole content; see docs/COR312_ANALYSIS.md). The comparison is with the hull of the union of possible images; domination by a single image is sufficient, not necessary.

      Ambiguities.

      • 'perhaps only up to some sort of approximation' (page 184) leaves the sense of 'construction of −|log(q)|' unspecified; the inclusion requires an exact statement.
      • Assessment: at the level of real numbers the premise of the last step is the conclusion; any non-circular argument must establish the inclusion at the level of regions or of the algorithm.
      • The inclusion is equivalent to the inequality conjunct only; with −|log(Θ)| = +∞ it holds trivially, so finiteness must be proved separately.

      Pages read: text; rendered and checked: page 184

    • IUT3.Cor3_12.StepXI.h (part or step) interpretation ambiguous blocked by external theorem
      Record

      The argument depends essentially on [EtTh], which admits no evident generalization to N-th tensor powers of Θ-pilot objects for N ≥ 2: the log-volume of such a power must be computed as N times that of the original. The analogue for N-th powers would give sharper inequalities, which are false (IUTchIV Rem 2.3.2 (ii)).

      Conclusion.

      Nature of the justification in the text: assertion with citation.

      Obligations.

      Ambiguities.

      • The text does not point to which substep uses N = 1; the dependence on [EtTh] is stated but not located.

      Pages read: text; rendered and checked: page 185

  • IUT3.Cor3_12.StepXII (part or step) extracted extracted
    Record

    Global realified Frobenioids are needed: the Θ^{×μ}_LGP-link preserves only isomorphism classes of objects of Frobenioids (Rem 3.6.2 (i)); with local Frobenioids one could not tell whether an object at v ∈ V^non corresponds to a submodule of the log-shell or to its p_v^N-multiple, because local objects admit endomorphisms that are not automorphisms, which would make a precise log-volume computation meaningless.

    Explicit hypotheses.

    • inherits the hypotheses of IUT3.Cor3_12.Statement

    Conclusion.

    Global realified Frobenioids are required: with local Frobenioids alone, objects are known only up to isomorphism class and admit non-invertible endomorphisms, so a submodule could not be distinguished from its p_v^N-multiple and a precise log-volume computation would be meaningless. Justification in the text: argument.

    Obligations.

    • Prove that isomorphism classes of objects of the global realified Frobenioid determine the degree (log-volume), while those of local Frobenioids do not determine the p_v^N scaling.

    Pages read: text; rendered and checked: pages 185, 186; text: pages 175 to 186

IUT3.Rem3_12_1Remark 3.12.1
Record

(i) IUTchIV seeks explicit upper bounds C_Θ. (ii) For λ ∈ Q_{>0}, generalized Θ^{×μ}_LGP-links q^λ ↦ q^{(1², ..., (l*)²)} give a similar theory, and 'one may apply the same arguments' to conclude C_Θ ≥ −λ; this is sharper for λ < 1 but irrelevant to IUTchIV, whose bounds are unaffected by a constant of absolute value ≤ 1. (iii) mono-theta cyclotomic rigidity as a 'canonical profinite volume' associated to the first power.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • λ ∈ Q_{>0}, and the generalized Θ^{×μ}_LGP-links q^λ ↦ q^{(1², ..., (l*)²)} (part (ii), page 186)

Conclusion.

C_Θ ≥ −λ for generalized links (asserted).

Obligations.

  • Check which steps of the proof change when the q-pilot is replaced by q^λ.

The text distinguishes the two (power on the q side versus on the theta side); a formalization must check that the q-side generalization uses no step that (xi-h) excludes.

IUT3.Rem3_12_2Remark 3.12.2
Record

(i) dismantling. (ii) logical structure a^itw to f^itw: (q-itw) ⇒ (q-itw) ∧ (Θ-itw/indets) ⇒ (Θ-itw/indets); toy model: λ_q : * ↦ −h ∈ ^qR, λ_Θ : * ↦ −2h ∈ ^ΘR, λ_Θ^Ind : * ↦ R_{≤−2h+ε}; if λ_Θ^Ind can be constructed as intrinsically associated to λ_q (simultaneously with, without invalidating, its conditions) even after identifying the copies of R, then −h ∈ R_{≤−2h+ε}, i.e., h ≤ ε. (iii) juggling of ⊞, ⊠ by log-links gives Ind3. (iv), (v) comparison of the 0- and 1-columns (Fig. 3.9).

Conclusion.

Interpretation of the logical structure of Step (xi).

Obligations.

  • The toy model's final step −h ∈ R_{≤−2h+ε} ⇔ h ≤ ε is elementary (same shape as stepXIf_inclusion_iff_inequality).
  • The content lies in (e^toy): the supposition that λ_Θ^Ind is intrinsically associated to λ_q. A formalization must state this supposition as a hypothesis and the corresponding property of the multiradial algorithm as the obligation.

Ambiguities.

  • In (e^toy) the property that does the work is introduced by 'suppose that one verifies'; the toy model does not exhibit the verification.

IUT4.ThmATheorem ADiophantine Inequalities
Record

Diophantine inequality for hyperbolic curves: the height of omega_X(D) is bounded, up to a bounded discrepancy, by (1 + epsilon) times the sum of the log-different and the log-conductor on points of bounded degree.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • X a smooth, proper, geometrically connected curve over a number field
  • D a reduced divisor, U_X = X minus D hyperbolic (deg omega_X(D) > 0)
  • d a positive integer, epsilon a positive real

Implicit hypotheses.

  • heights, log-differents, log-conductors in the sense of [GenEll], Definitions 1.2 and 1.5
  • the relation <~ in the corrected sense of Remark 2.3.1 (ii)

Conclusion.

ht_{omega_X(D)} <~ (1 + epsilon)(log-diff_X + log-cond_D) on U_X(Qbar)^{<= d}.

Obligations.

  • Theorem A is Corollary 2.3; see that record.
  • The specialization to the abc inequality (X = P^1, D = {0, 1, infinity}, d = 1) is described in docs/PAPER_IV_DERIVATION.md and is not formalized.

Pages read: text and render of page 3

IUT4.Prop1_1Proposition 1.1Multiple Tensor Products and Differents
Record

Multiple tensor products and differents. For a fixed index * in I and I* = I minus {}, the normalization (R_I)^~ of the tensor product R_I = tensor over Z_p of the R_i satisfies p^{d_{I}} (R_I)^~ inside R_I inside (R_I)^~, where d_{I*} is the sum of the orders d_i of the differents over i in I*.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • p a prime number; I a finite set of cardinality >= 2
  • for i in I, k_i a finite extension of Q_p inside an algebraic closure of Q_p, R_i its ring of integers, d_i in Q_{>=0} the order of a generator of the different ideal of R_i over Z_p
  • for nonempty E in I: R_E = tensor product over Z_p of the R_i (i in E), d_E = sum of d_i (i in E)
  • a fixed element * of I, and I* = I minus {*}

Implicit hypotheses.

  • ord is the p-adic valuation normalized by ord(p) = 1; p^lambda denotes an unspecified element of order lambda
  • (-)^~ is the normalization of the reduced ring in its ring of fractions

Conclusion.

p^{d_{I*}} (R_I)^~ is contained in R_I, which is contained in (R_I)^~; the left-hand side is well defined for suitable p^{d_{I*}}, for example products of elements p^{d_i} of R_i (i in I*).

Obligations.

  • Not formalized: needs tensor products of p-adic rings of integers and their normalizations, which Mathlib does not provide in this form.

Pages read: renders of pages 9 and 10

extracted		none
IUT4.Prop1_2Proposition 1.2Differents and Logarithms
Record

Defines a_i = (1/e_i) ceil(e_i/(p-2)) (p > 2), a_i = 2 (p = 2), b_i = floor(log(p e_i/(p-1))/log p) − 1/e_i, and bounds p-adic logarithms of unit groups and automorphisms of their tensor products by powers of p.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • the notation of Proposition 1.1 (p, I, k_i, R_i, d_i)
  • e_i the ramification index of k_i over Q_p
  • phi an automorphism of the Q_p-vector space log_p(R_I^x) tensor Q_p that induces an automorphism of the submodule log_p(R_I^x)
  • lambda in (1/e_i) Z, i in I (in (ii), (iii))
  • (iii): p > 2 and e_i <= p - 2 for all i; (iv): p > 2 and e_i = 1 for all i

Conclusion.

(i) p^{a_i} R_i in log_p(R_i^x) in p^{-b_i} R_i, with equalities if p > 2 and e_i <= p-2; (ii) phi(p^lambda (R_I)^~) in p^{floor(lambda - d_I - a_I)} log_p(R_I^x) in p^{floor(lambda - d_I - a_I) - b_I} (R_I)^~; (iii) phi(p^lambda (R_I)^~) in p^{lambda - d_I - 1} (R_I)^~; (iv) phi((R_I)^~) in (R_I)^~.

Obligations.

  • The inclusions (i) to (iv) need the p-adic logarithm on unit groups and tensor products of rings of integers; not formalized.
  • Elementary facts about a_i, b_i used in the proof are checked in the fragments listed in fragments.

Ambiguities.

  • The text extraction drops the ceiling in a_i and the floor in b_i; the render of page 10 shows both.

Pages read: renders of pages 10 and 11

IUT4.Prop1_3Proposition 1.3Estimates of Differents
Record

Estimates of differents: d_i >= d_0 + (e_{i/0} − 1)/e_i, with equality if tame; and d_i <= d_0 + n_i + 1/e_0 when k_i is Galois over a tame extension of k_0.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • the notation of Proposition 1.2; k_0 a subfield of k_i containing Q_p, R_0 its ring of integers, d_0 the order of a generator of its different, e_0 its ramification index, e_{i/0} = e_i/e_0, n_i the p-adic valuation of [k_i : k_0]
  • (ii): k_i finite Galois over a subfield k_1 with k_0 in k_1 and k_1 tamely ramified over k_0

Conclusion.

(i) d_i >= d_0 + (e_{i/0} - 1)/(e_{i/0} e_0) = d_0 + (e_{i/0} - 1)/e_i, with equality if k_i is tamely ramified over k_0; (ii) d_i <= d_0 + n_i + 1/e_0.

Obligations.

  • The local field inputs (Eisenstein and Kummer polynomials, differents, ramification in towers) are not formalized.
  • Arithmetic steps of the proof are checked in the fragments listed in fragments.

IUT4.Prop1_4Proposition 1.4Nonarchimedean Normalized Log-volume Estimates
Record

Normalized log-volume estimates for p-adic tensor packets: normalization of mu^log, the value of mu^log(log_p(R_i^x)), and upper bounds for the log-volumes of the regions containing phi(p^lambda (R_I)^~).

Explicit hypotheses.

  • the notation of Proposition 1.2; R_i^mu the torsion of R_i^x, p^{f_i} the residue field cardinality, p^{m_i} the order of the p-primary part of R_i^mu
  • (iii): I* a subset of I with p - 2 >= e_i for all i in I minus I*; lambda in (1/e_{i_dagger}) Z, i_dagger in I
  • (iv): p > 2 and e_i = 1 for all i in I

Conclusion.

(i) a normalized log-volume mu^log on compact open subsets of finite extensions of Q_p and of their tensor products, with mu^log(R_i) = 0, mu^log(p R_i) = -log p, mu^log((R_E)^~) = 0, mu^log(p (R_E)^~) = -log p; (ii) mu^log(log_p(R_i^x)) = -(1/e_i + m_i/(e_i f_i)) log p; (iii) the inclusions phi(p^lambda (R_I)^~) in p^{floor(lambda - d_I - a_I)} log_p(R_I^x) in p^{floor(lambda - d_I - a_I) - b_I} (R_I)^~, the bounds mu^log(p^{floor(lambda-d_I-a_I)} log_p(R_I^x)) <= (-lambda + d_I + 1 + 4|I*|/p) log p and mu^log(p^{floor(lambda-d_I-a_I)-b_I} (R_I)^~) <= (-lambda + d_I + 1) log p + sum_{i in I*} {3 + log e_i}, and d_I + a_I >= |I| (p > 2), >= 2|I| (p = 2); (iv) phi((R_I)^~) in (R_I)^~ and mu^log((R_I)^~) = 0.

Obligations.

  • The log-volume mu^log of [AbsTopIII], Proposition 5.7, its normalization (i), its value (ii) and the inclusions of (iii) and (iv) are not formalized.
  • The fragments check the arithmetic of the proof starting from supplied volume bounds; none of them establishes the measure normalization or the containments.

Ambiguities.

  • The first display of (iii) writes p^{lambda − d_I − a_I}; the render shows a floor, which accounts for the +1.

IUT4.Prop1_5Proposition 1.5Archimedean Metric Estimates
Record

Archimedean metric estimates: C tensor_R C = C + C is compatible with metrics up to a factor 2; primitive automorphisms preserve decompositions; M_I splits into 2^{|I|-1}|V|^{|I|} copies of C with metric factor 2^{|I|-1}; tensor products of vectors of length lambda lie in lambda^{|I|} B_I.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • C with its standard Hermitian metric; primitive automorphisms generated by complex conjugation and multiplication by +-1, +-sqrt(-1)
  • (iii), (iv): I, V nonempty finite sets; M = direct sum of copies C_v (v in V); M_I = tensor product over R of copies M_i of M; B_I the product of unit balls
  • (iv): lambda > 0 and m_i in M_i whose components have length lambda

Conclusion.

(i) C tensor_R C = C + C is compatible with the metrics up to a factor 2; (ii) the decomposition is preserved by primitive automorphisms; (iii) M_I is a direct sum of 2^{|I|-1}|V|^{|I|} copies of C, with direct sum metric 2^{|I|-1} times the tensor metric, and B_I is preserved; (iv) the tensor product of the m_i lies in lambda^{|I|} B_I.

Obligations.

  • Tensor product metrics, the primitive automorphisms of (ii), the metric factor and integral structure of (iii) and the inclusion of (iv) are not formalized.

Pages read: text of pages 14 to 16; renders of pages 15 and 16

    • v : N

IUT4.Prop1_6Proposition 1.6The Prime Number Theorem
Record

Consequence of the prime number theorem: n <= 4 p_n / (3 log p_n) for n large, and pi(eta) <= 4 eta/(3 log eta) for eta >= eta_prm.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • p_n the n-th smallest prime (p_1 = 2), n a positive integer

Conclusion.

There is n_0 with n <= 4 p_n/(3 log p_n) for all n >= n_0; there is a positive real eta_prm with sum_{p <= eta} 1 <= 4 eta/(3 log eta) for all real eta >= eta_prm.

Obligations.

  • proposition1_6 states both displays with one witness each, proved from Mathlib's Nat.isEquivalent_primeCounting; exists_n0, exists_eta_prm and counting_of_nth (the second display follows formally from the first) are the components.

Ambiguities.

  • The Lean statement gives eta_prm >= 2, stronger than the paper's 'positive'. Positivity suffices for Theorem 1.10: Step (viii) divides by log(eta_prm) only when 1 < e*_mod l < eta_prm (page 31).

Pages read: render of page 16

IUT4.Prop1_8Proposition 1.8Torsion Points of Elliptic Curves
Record

Torsion points of elliptic curves over a perfect field k: Serre's criterion; the exact sequence of automorphism groups, the minimal field of definition and models; the 2-torsion representation; uniqueness of models with rational l-torsion; semistable reduction from rational l-torsion; the Legendre form; ramification of torsion fields.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • k a perfect field, kbar an algebraic closure, G_k = Gal(kbar/k)
  • (i): l >= 3 a prime invertible in k, k = kbar, A an abelian variety over k with a polarization lambda
  • (ii): E_kbar an elliptic curve over kbar with origin; n a positive integer
  • (iii): as (ii), with Aut_kbar(E_kbar) = {+-1}
  • (iv): as (ii), l >= 3 a prime invertible in k, E'_k and E''_k models over k with all l-torsion rational over k
  • (v): as (ii), k a complete discrete valuation field with ring of integers O_k, l >= 3 a prime invertible in O_k, E_k a model over k with all l-torsion rational
  • (vi): as (iii), 2 invertible in k, G_E = G_k, and G_E -> Aut(E_kbar[2]) trivial; for the second part k a complete discrete valuation field with 2 invertible in O_k
  • (vii): as (ii), k a complete discrete valuation field, E_k a model over k, n invertible in O_k

Conclusion.

(i) Aut_k(A, lambda) -> Aut(A[l]) is injective; (ii) an exact sequence 1 -> Aut_kbar(E_kbar) -> Aut_k(E_kbar) -> G_k with open image G_E, a representation rho_n on E_kbar[n], k_E = field of definition = k(j), and models over k_H correspond to sections over H; (iii) rho_2 factors through G_E -> Aut(E_kbar[2]); (iv) E'_k is isomorphic to E''_k; (v) E_k has semistable reduction over O_k; (vi) E_kbar descends to E_k over k in Legendre form, which has semistable reduction over O_k' for some k'/k with [k' : k] <= 2, and k' = k is possible if E_k has good reduction over O_k'; (vii) good reduction gives G_k acting unramified on E_kbar[n], and bad multiplicative reduction gives a tamely ramified extension with ramification index dividing n.

Obligations.

  • Needs elliptic curves over local fields, torsion fields and Neron models.

Pages read: renders of pages 18 to 21

pages 18 to 21extracted
blocked by mathlib		none
IUT4.Def1_9Definition 1.9
Record

(i) arithmetic divisors, deg_F with weights log(q_v) and 1, normalized degree; (ii) for a set E of valuations over one v_Q, deg_E(a) = deg(a_E)/sum_{v in E}[F_v : Q_{v_Q}], invariant under passing to finite extensions.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • F a number field; V(F) its valuations
  • (ii): v_Q a valuation of Q and E a nonempty subset of V(F) of valuations lying over v_Q
  • K a finite extension of F (for the invariance statements)

Conclusion.

Definitions of ADiv_R(F), deg_F, the normalized degree, a_E and deg_E(a) = deg(a_E) / sum_{v in E} [F_v : Q_{v_Q}], together with the invariance statements deg(a|K) = deg(a) and deg{E|K}(a|_K) = deg_E(a).

Obligations.

  • Not formalized: pull-back of arithmetic divisors along a finite extension K/F, the set E|K, and the invariance statements deg(a|K) = deg(a) of (i) and deg{E|K}(a|_K) = deg_E(a) of (ii).
  • Not proved: that the local degrees over all valuations above one finite v_Q sum to [F : Q] (proved only for the archimedean place).

Ambiguities.

  • In the render of page 22 the line under deg(a_E) is the fraction bar, so the numerator is the unnormalized deg_F of (i); the Lean definition uses deg_F.

IUT4.Thm1_10Theorem 1.10Log-volume Estimates for Θ-Pilot Objects
Record

Log-volume estimates for Theta-pilot objects. For initial Theta-data in the situation of IUT III Corollary 3.12, with E_F of good reduction at the good nonarchimedean valuations not dividing 2l, the tripodal field F_tpd = F_mod(E_{F_mod}[2]), the (3 5)-torsion of E_F rational over F and F = F_mod(sqrt(-1), E_{F_mod}[2 3 5]): the real number (l+1)/(4|log(q)|) {(1 + 12 d_mod/l)(log(d^{F_tpd}) + log(f^{F_tpd})) + 10(e*_mod l + eta_prm) - (1/6)(1 - 12/l^2) log(q-frak)} - 1 may be taken as the constant C_Theta of IUT III Corollary 3.12 (that is, it is an admissible real bound in the sense of that corollary), and hence, by C_Theta >= -1, (1/6) log(q-frak) <= (1 + 20 d_mod/l)(log(d^{F_tpd}) + log(f^{F_tpd})) + 20(e*_mod l + eta_prm) <= (1 + 20 d_mod/l)(log(d^F) + log(f^F)) + 20(e*_mod l + eta_prm). Here |log(q)| = log(q-frak)/(2l).

Explicit hypotheses.

  • a collection of initial Theta-data as in IUTchI Definition 3.1, in the situation of IUTchIII Corollary 3.12
  • E_F has good reduction at every valuation in V(F)^good intersect V(F)^non that does not divide 2l
  • d_mod = [F_mod : Q]; e_mod (1 <= e_mod <= d_mod) the maximal ramification index of F_mod over Q; d*_mod = 2^12 3^3 5 d_mod, e*_mod = 2^12 3^3 5 e_mod
  • F_mod in F_tpd = F_mod(E_{F_mod}[2]) in F, the tripodal field obtained by adjoining the fields of definition of the 2-torsion of any model of E_F over F_mod
  • the (3 5)-torsion points of E_F are defined over F
  • F = F_mod(sqrt(-1), E_{F_mod}[2 3 5]) = F_tpd(sqrt(-1), E_{F_tpd}[3 5]), with E_{F_tpd} the model given by the Legendre form
  • d^{F_box}, q^{F_box}, f^{F_box} the arithmetic divisors of the different, the q-parameters at V(F_box)^bad, and the conductor, and their log-degrees as on page 23; eta_prm the positive real number of Proposition 1.6

Implicit hypotheses.

  • l is a prime >= 5 (IUTchI Definition 3.1) and l != 5 (noted on page 22 from IUTchI Definition 3.1 (c)); hence l >= 7, which the final estimate uses
  • |log(q)| > 0 is the quantity of IUTchIII Corollary 3.12, equal to log(q-frak)/(2l) (page 23)

Conclusion.

The displayed expression is an admissible value of C_Theta for IUT III Corollary 3.12, and the two-line final inequality for (1/6) log(q-frak) holds.

Obligations.

  • The local inputs (Propositions 1.1 to 1.5, 1.8), (E5), the construction of IUT III Theorem 3.11 and IUT III Corollary 3.12 are not formalized.
  • The arithmetic of the steps is checked in the fragments of the step records; the displayed value of C_Theta is the definition of the fragment IUT4.Thm1_10.frag.constant.

Ambiguities.

  • The Lean definition constant keeps |log(q)| (variable q) and log(q-frak) (variable Q) as separate arguments; the relation q = Q/(2l) of page 23 is a hypothesis of the lemmas that need it.

Pages read: renders of pages 22 to 31

    Record

    Sum of the local bounds, the final display of Step (iii), the estimate (l+1)/4 (1/6)(12/l^2) >= 1/(2l), the prime count l*_mod log s^<= <= (4/3)(e*_mod l + eta_prm) from Proposition 1.6, absorption of 2 log l + 56, giving the stated C_Theta.

    Explicit hypotheses.

    • eta_prm as in Proposition 1.6
    • l >= 5, e_mod >= 1

    Conclusion.

    C_Theta |log(q)| is at most (l+1)/4 {(1 + 12 d_mod/l)(log d + log f) + 2 log l + 56 - (1/6)(1 - 12/l^2) log q + (20/3) l*_mod log s^<=} - (1/(2l)) log q; l*_mod log(s^<=) <= (4/3)(e*_mod l + eta_prm); hence the value of C_Theta displayed in Theorem 1.10 is an upper bound.

    Obligations.

IUT4.Prop2_1Proposition 2.1Well-known Estimates
Record

(i) log x <= x for x >= 1. (ii) There is xi_prm >= 5 with (2/3)x <= theta(x) <= (4/3)x for x >= xi_prm; for a finite set A of primes there is a prime p not in A with p <= 2(theta_A + xi_prm).

Explicit hypotheses.

  • (ii): theta(x) = sum of log p over primes p <= x; A a finite set of primes, theta_A = sum of log p over A (0 if A is empty)

Conclusion.

(i) log x <= x for real x >= 1; (ii) there is xi_prm >= 5 with (2/3) x <= theta(x) <= (4/3) x for x >= xi_prm, and, with the same xi_prm, a prime p not in A with p <= 2(theta_A + xi_prm).

Obligations.

  • proposition2_1 states (i) and (ii) with a single xi_prm, proved from Mathlib's Chebyshev.tendsto_theta_div_atTop; log_le_self, exists_xi_prm, exists_prime_not_mem_of and exists_prime_not_mem are the components. A is any finite set of natural numbers in Lean, which includes the paper's sets of primes.

Ambiguities.

  • The extracted text reads 'p in A'; the render shows 'p not in A'.

IUT4.Cor2_2Corollary 2.2Construction of Suitable Initial Θ-Data
Record

Construction of initial Theta-data from a point of a compactly bounded subset K_V of the lambda-line satisfying (*_{j-inv}), with (C1) and (C2), and the uniform bound on exceptional sets.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • X = P^1 over Q, D = {0, 1, infinity}, regarded as the lambda-line of the Legendre form
  • K_V a compactly bounded subset of U_X(Qbar) (in the sense of Remark 2.3.1 (vi)) whose support contains the prime 2, satisfying (*_{j-inv})
  • (ii), (iii): d a positive integer and eps_d, eps real numbers in (0, 1]

Conclusion.

(i), (ii) with (C1), (C2), (iii).

Obligations.

  • The arithmetic of the proof is kernel checked in IUT/PaperIV/Corollary2_2/Arithmetic.lean; see the part and step records.

      Pages 47 and 48: an explicit admissible H_unif independent of K_V for the height bound.

      Not covered.

      The height bound on the exceptional set itself, which also involves H_K and the finiteness argument.

      Pages read: rendered page 47

      Explicit hypotheses.

      • 1 <= delta
      • 0 < eps_d <= 1
      • 0 < eps <= 1

      h < (eps (eps_d/16))^(-3) (60 delta)^(4 + eps_d).

IUT4.Cor2_3Corollary 2.3Diophantine Inequalities
Record

Diophantine inequalities: same statement as Theorem A. Proof: equivalent to [GenEll] Theorem 2.1 (i); by that theorem it suffices to prove (ii) of it, i.e. the inequality on K_V for the lambda-line, which follows from Corollary 2.2 (i), (ii), (iii) and d_mod <= d.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • X a smooth, proper, geometrically connected curve over a number field; D a reduced divisor; U_X = X minus D
  • d a positive integer, eps a positive real number
  • U_X hyperbolic: deg omega_X(D) > 0

Conclusion.

ht_{omega_X(D)} <~ (1 + eps)(log-diff_X + log-cond_D) on U_X(Qbar)^{<=d}, i.e. (1 + eps)(log-diff_X + log-cond_D) - ht_{omega_X(D)} is bounded below by a constant there.

Obligations.

  • Corollary 2.3 is not stated in Lean: heights, log-differents, log-conductors, curves and points of bounded degree are not formalized.
  • [GenEll] Theorem 2.1 (the reduction to the lambda-line) is external and not formalized.
  • The fragments check only the logical form of the last step on K_V, as abstract lemmas on real functions on an arbitrary set.

Ambiguities.

  • Corollary 2.2 (iii) assumes epsilon <= 1; for epsilon > 1 the statement follows from epsilon = 1 since log-diff + log-cond >= 0 (BDle_mono_eps).

    Record

    Abstract lemma: for any set S, a finite set Exc, real functions ht, H, Lambda, epsE and reals eps, C_K: if ht approx (1/6)H on S, (1/6)H <= (1 + epsE) Lambda + C_K and epsE <= eps on S minus Exc, and Lambda >= 0 on S, then ht <~ (1 + eps) Lambda on S.

    Explicit hypotheses.

    • S : Set alpha (any set, possibly empty)
    • Exc : Finset alpha
    • BDeq S ht ((1/6) H)
    • for x in S not in Exc: (1/6) H x <= (1 + epsE x) Lambda x + CK
    • for x in S not in Exc: epsE x <= eps
    • for x in S: 0 <= Lambda x

    Conclusion.

    BDle S ht ((1 + eps) Lambda).

    Covers.

Record

Corrections of misprints in [GenEll]: (i) ord_v is defined on F_v^x; (ii) in Definition 1.2 (ii) the two relations are swapped: alpha <~ beta means alpha(x) - beta(x) <= C, alpha >~ beta means beta(x) - alpha(x) <= C; (iii) the first sentence of Corollary 4.4 should begin 'Let Qbar be an algebraic closure of Q'; (iv) a log-diff term in Corollary 4.4 is corrected; (v) the final BD equality in the proof of Theorem 2.1 should be ht_E <~ 2 (deg E/deg omega_X) ht_{omega_X}, with the corresponding factor 2 in the choice of eps'; (vi) the meaning of a compactly bounded subset of U(Qbar): its local domains lie in U; (vii) X(Q_v) should read X(Qbar_v) in Example 1.3 (ii); (viii) a definition of |alpha|^2 should use the absolute value of the integral.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • the notation of [GenEll]

Conclusion.

The eight corrections (i) to (viii) to [GenEll].

Obligations.

  • Only the corrected relation <~ of (ii) has a Lean counterpart (fragment BDle); the other corrections concern [GenEll] and are not formalized.

IUT4.Def3_1Definition 3.1
Record

Species: a 0-species is a collection of conditions on a set expressible in ZFC; a 1-species is a 0-species of morphisms together with composition; a species is a 0-species and a 1-species defining a category in every ZFC-model; specimens, i-smallness, species-isomorphisms, the category determined by a species, model-free use.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • set-theoretic formulas are absolute in the sense stated on page 68 (validity unchanged between universes V in W)
  • (ii): a 0-species S_0; (iii): a 0-species S_0 and a 1-species S_1 acting on it

Conclusion.

Definitions of (i) 0-species, collections of species-data, 0-specimens, 0-smallness; (ii) 1-species acting on a 0-species via formulas Phi_1 and Phi_{1o1} with unique composites, associativity and identities in every ZFC-model, 1-specimens, 1-smallness; (iii) species, species-objects and species-morphisms, i-smallness, species-isomorphisms and automorphisms, model-free specimens; (iv) the category determined by a species in a ZFC-model.

Obligations.

  • A definition: nothing to prove. Species are not formalized.

IUT4.Def3_3Definition 3.3
Record

Mutations between species S, S': set-theoretic formulas Psi_0, Psi_1 assigning to each specimen of S a unique specimen of S', compatible with composites and identities (so a functor in every ZFC-model); morphisms of mutations (natural transformations), isomorphisms, mutation-equivalences and quasi-inverses; mutation-histories indexed by oriented graphs; covariant and contravariant observables of a mutation-history over a simply connected graph, and cores.

Explicit hypotheses.

  • S = (S_0, S_1) and S' = (S'_0, S'_1) species (Definition 3.1)
  • (ii): mutations M, M' : S ~> S'
  • (iii): a mutation M : S ~> S'; for the bijection statement, S and S' 1-small
  • (iv): an oriented graph (underlying graph with a total order on the branches of each edge)
  • (v): a mutation-history H whose underlying graph is simply connected, and a species S'

Conclusion.

Definitions of mutations, composition and identity mutations, morphisms and isomorphisms of mutations, mutation-equivalences (with the observation that for 1-small species they induce bijections on species-morphisms and species-isomorphisms), mutation-histories, observables and cores.

Obligations.

  • A definition: nothing to prove, except the observation in (iii). Mutations are not formalized.

IUT1.Sec0.AbstractlyEquivalentdefinition
Record

Isomorphism classes of functors φ : C_1 → C_2 and φ' : C'_1 → C'_2 are abstractly equivalent if there are isomorphisms α_i : C_i ⥲ C'_i (i = 1, 2) with φ' ∘ α_1 = α_2 ∘ φ; the same terminology applies to morphisms of (connected) temperoids and of their subcategories of connected objects.

Conclusion.

Definition (no proof obligation).

IUT1.Sec0.Capsuledefinition
Record

A capsule of objects of a category C is a finite collection {A_j}_{j∈J} of objects of C indexed by a finite index set J; it is a |J|-capsule, and π_0({A_j}_{j∈J}) = J.

Conclusion.

Definition (no proof obligation).

Obligations.

  • The Lean declaration is a definition; there is no proof to verify.
  • The |J|-capsule terminology and π_0 are IUT.PaperI.Capsule.IsNCapsule and IUT.PaperI.Capsule.π₀.

Ambiguities.

  • The index set is a Lean type in Type with a Fintype instance.

IUT1.Sec0.CapsuleFullPolyMorphismdefinition
Record

A capsule-full poly-morphism {A_j}{j∈J} → {A'{j'}}_{j'∈J'} is the poly-morphism associated to a fixed injection ι : J ↪ J' consisting of the capsule morphisms given by collections of arbitrary isomorphisms A_j ⥲ A'_{ι(j)}, j ∈ J.

Conclusion.

Definition (no proof obligation).

Obligations.

  • The Lean declaration is a definition; there is no proof to verify.

IUT1.Sec0.CapsuleMorphismdefinition
Record

A morphism of capsules {A_j}{j∈J} → {A'{j'}}_{j'∈J'} consists of an injection ι : J ↪ J' and, for each j ∈ J, a morphism A_j → A'_{ι(j)}; capsules of objects of C form a category Capsule(C).

Conclusion.

Definition (no proof obligation).

Obligations.

  • IUT.PaperI.Capsule.Hom, Hom.id and Hom.comp state the morphisms, identities and composition, but the category laws of Capsule(C) are not proved in Lean, so this record is not linked to a Lean declaration.

IUT1.Sec0.CategoryIsomorphismdefinition
Record

For categories C, D, an isomorphism C → D means an isomorphism class of equivalences of categories C → D (differing from standard terminology); equivalently an isomorphism in the coarsification of the 2-category of small 1-categories relative to a suitable universe.

Conclusion.

Definition (no proof obligation).

IUT1.Sec0.PolyMorphismdefinition
Record

For objects A, B of a category C, a poly-morphism A → B is a collection of morphisms A → B, that is, a subset of the set of morphisms A → B.

Conclusion.

IUT1.Sec0.PolyMorphismCompositedefinition
Record

The composite of a poly-morphism {f_i : A → B}_{i∈I} with a poly-morphism {g_j : B → C}_{j∈J} is the poly-morphism given by the set {g_j ∘ f_i}_{(i,j)∈I×J}, multiplicities ignored.

Conclusion.

Definition (no proof obligation).

Obligations.

  • The Lean declaration is a definition; there is no proof to verify.

Ambiguities.

  • Lean composes in diagrammatic order: g_j ∘ f_i is f_i ≫ g_j.

IUT1.Sec0.PseudoMonoiddefinition
Record

A topological pseudo-monoid is a topological space P with a continuous map S → P, S ⊆ P × P, such that there are a topological abelian group M (written multiplicatively) and an embedding ι : P ↪ M with S = {(a, b) | ι(a)·ι(b) ∈ ι(P)} and S → P the restriction of the group law. With M discrete, P is a pseudo-monoid. P is divisible if M, ι can be chosen so that every element of M has n-th roots for all n ≥ 1 and a ∈ ι(P) if and only if a^n ∈ ι(P); P is cyclotomic if M, ι can be chosen with μ_M ≅ Q/Z, μ_M ⊆ ι(P) and μ_M·ι(P) ⊆ ι(P).

Conclusion.

Definition (no proof obligation).

Pages read: 33 to 36 (rendered images)

page 33extracted
not extracted		none
IUT1.Sec0.TemperedGroupdefinition
Record

For a topological group Π, B^temp(Π) is the category of countable discrete sets with continuous Π-action and Π-equivariant maps. Π is tempered if it is an inverse limit of an inverse system of surjections of countable discrete topological groups. A category equivalent to B^temp(Π) with Π tempered is a connected temperoid; then C ≃ (C^0)^⊤. For Galois-countable Π, Π is reconstructed up to inner automorphism from B^temp(Π) or B^temp(Π)^0, which defines π_1(C), π_1(C^0) for a Galois-countable connected temperoid C.

Conclusion.

Definition (no proof obligation).

Obligations.

  • Tempered groups and temperoids are not in Mathlib; the reconstruction of π_1 cites Remark 2.5.3 (i), (T1), (T5).

Pages read: 33 to 36 (rendered images)

not extracted		none
IUT1.Sec0.TemperoidMorphismdefinition
Record

For connected temperoids C_1, C_2, a morphism C_1 → C_2 is an isomorphism class of functors C_2 → C_1 preserving finite limits and countable colimits; a morphism C_1^0 → C_2^0 is a morphism (C_1^0)^⊤ → (C_2^0)^⊤. For Galois-countable tempered Π_1, Π_2 there are natural bijections between continuous outer homomorphisms Π_1 → Π_2, morphisms B^temp(Π_1) → B^temp(Π_2), and morphisms B^temp(Π_1)^0 → B^temp(Π_2)^0.

Conclusion.

Definition (no proof obligation).

Obligations.

  • Temperoids are not in Mathlib.

Ambiguities.

  • Page 35 notes this differs slightly from [SemiAnbd] Definition 3.1 (iii).

not extracted		none
IUT1.Sec0.Valuationsdefinition
Record

NF, MLF, CAF abbreviate number field, mixed-characteristic nonarchimedean local field, complex archimedean field; Primes is the set of prime numbers. For a number field F, V(F) = V(F)^arc ∪ V(F)^non is the set of valuations (places) of F; F_v is the completion at v; an element is integral at v if of norm ≤ 1; for a Galois extension L of F, L_v is the completion at a valuation of L over v; p_v is the residue characteristic for nonarchimedean v and, for archimedean v, the positive real element of F_v with natural logarithm 1 (p_v = e). These notations extend to infinite extensions of Q by projective or inductive limits.

Conclusion.

Definition (no proof obligation).
